- **🎯 FULL LAZY CoW — the single mental model (owner-confirmed 2026-07-02; continuation of Feb-22 immutable-borrow-args `22b6b53b` + May-31 unify-CoW `eea27a2f` + Jun-9 materialize-on-mutation `2ffa8882`).** A resource value is a cheap borrow until a write reaches it; the write MATERIALIZES a private copy at the closest immutable context — **lazy** (deferred to the write, never speculative; a mutation that never runs never allocates) — and lands there; `&`/`&self`/`for x in &coll` write through to the real owner. Model + detailed gap-map: session scratch `cow_mutation_model.md`. Docs landed (`language-design §3`, `book/11-12`, `devbook/11`, `reference §9.6`). **MEASURED clone delta on the bounded fix = ZERO** (self-host is `&`-correct → materialize path unexercised).   - **🐛 `for x in &coll` element write-through is LOST — even on an OWNED vector (filed round-34 by the G2 track; NOT a G2 materialize gap, a distinct for-loop `&`-binding write-through hole).** The mutation inside the loop body never reaches the collection's backing store. Repro (prints `1`, SHOULD print `101`):
    ```
    struct Cell:
        int n
    void main():
        Vector[Cell] a = [Cell(1), Cell(2)]
        for c in &a:            # &-bound loop var = exclusive write-through per the CoW model
            c.n = c.n + 100
        print(a[0].n)           # got 1, expected 101
    ```
    Aliased-root variant (`Vector[Cell] b = a; for c in &b: c.n += 100`) likewise leaves BOTH `a` and `b` unchanged. Fix is in the for-loop lowering (the `&`-bound element local is a copy, not a write-through pointer into the collection element) — NOT the `&`-formation materialize sites G2 touches. **⚡ SCOUTED 2026-07-16 (CoW-1A scout, report `docs/plans/cow-1a-scout.md` + proto `/tmp/cow1a_proto.patch`): CONFIRMED BOTH-COMPILER (baseline self-host == Rust, both wrong → self-host-only fix = parity REGRESSION, NO-GO); + NEW gap A2 (Rust BARE `for c in v` resource-elem WRITES THROUGH, violates §3.1 — self-host correct); + the alias-root sever is the hard Rust case. ALREADY filed in detail at the `async_task_group` 5-layer-fix item (line ~155) + the KEYSTONE spawn-opaque-param lever — cross-ref; the R42-D scout re-confirmed the root.

- **🆕 ROUND-39 T2 spawn/blocking follow-ups (filed at landing per brief; the +5 fixtures now MATCH, these are the residual semantic gaps — each output-invisible on the current corpus):**
  - **(1) Synchronous inline-closure-spawn model.** The self-host lowers `spawn ((): body)()` (inline closure literal) SYNCHRONOUSLY — the closure body runs on the CURRENT thread at the spawn site and the paired `.await()` is a no-op (the `case EClosure` arm in `lower_expr.gg` ESpawn→ECall now dispatches `__callable_<arity>` inline, same model as the already-passing `spawn_closure_copy`/`spawn_closure_void` closure-VAR path). Rust threads it via `build_spawn_wrapper` (a real spawned thread). CORRECT-OUTPUT ONLY for concurrency-agnostic fixtures — the 3 that landed (`spawn_closure_inline`/`spawn_unchecked`/`spawn_unchecked_bypasses_check`) all await immediately after spawn, so output is deterministic regardless of sync-vs-thread. A fixture whose output depends on real parallelism/interleaving would be right-by-luck. Fix = materialize the inline closure into a real spawn wrapper (mirror Rust `build_spawn_wrapper`); pairs with the Group-A pool gap below.
  - **(2) Anonymous awaited-String-temp leak (14B on `spawn_blocking_multi`) — PRE-EXISTING in the shared real-spawn/await path, NOT introduced by T2, NOT blocking-specific.** `print(t.await())` on a `Task[String]` leaks 14 bytes in 3 allocations (`str_alloc_copy` ← `gorget_file_read_all` ← `blocking_read`, via `__gorget_global_alloc_fn`): the awaited-String temp passed DIRECTLY to a call arg is never drop-registered. It is the anonymous-temp shape that leaks, NOT the await count — `spawn_blocking_basic` (`String result = t.await(); print(result)`) is ASan-CLEAN because the awaited result binds to a NAMED local that IS drop-registered (`gorget_string_free(&result)` emitted). **Re-verified 2026-07-04 by rebuilding the committed driver + ASan matrix: basic CLEAN, multi 14B → 4/5 clean.** The 14B reproduces IDENTICALLY in a regular (NON-blocking) `spawn areader(...)` awaited inline in `print` (`reg_inline` repro), and vanishes when that same regular spawn binds the result to a named local (`reg_named` clean) — so the leak lives in the shared await-result-temp lowering, exercised by both regular `spawn` and `spawn blocking`. Same CLASS as the already-filed trait-call-return-value temp leak (`print(s.speak())`, in this file's ROLLING queue item 6) — a call/await result temp consumed as an arg isn't drop-registered. Fix = drop-register the owned result temp at the consuming call-arg / auto-prop site (must be conditional to avoid a double-free when the result also binds to a VarDecl — deep drop-machinery, not a spawn-local patch; do NOT chase in a spawn track). ⚠ A pre-fix bare-passthrough driver mis-attributes this as a 21B `spawn_blocking_basic` "sync-inline" leak — that is the OLD behavior (direct `blocking_read` call, no spawn runtime); rebuild the driver from the committed `lower_expr.gg` before measuring.
  - **(3) Group-A blocking-pool routing divergence.** T2 routes `spawn blocking f()` through the SAME `lower_named_fn_spawn` wiring that regular `spawn` uses — main() emits REAL spawn/await (`__gorget_spawn_<fn>` → `GORGET_SCHEDULER_SUBMIT` on a pthread-backed `GorgetTask`, `__gorget_await_<fn>` → `GORGET_SCHEDULER_WAIT`; NOT sync-inline), but on the SAME fixed-size executor with no blocking-pool accounting. Rust routes it to an EXPANDABLE blocking pool (`blocking_fn_names`/`has_blocking_pool` → `BLOCKING_POOL_RUNTIME`). OUTPUT-INVISIBLE for the 5 fixtures (the emitted VALUE is identical — the awaited real return value — so the flip is reference-grade on OUTPUT), but a latent LIVENESS gap: fixed-executor saturation under many concurrent blocking tasks could deadlock where Rust's expandable pool would not. Fix = give the self-host a blocking-pool tag + expandable-pool runtime, or document the fixed-executor semantics as intentional.

- **🆕 CI/TOOLING FOLLOW-UPS (filed 2026-06-30, from the `test_result_advanced` CI-red fix — see DONE):**
  - **🔧 macOS dev machine CANNOT run the self-host runtime net / bootstrap locally without a `cc` shim — this is WHY the `Result.map` drop-corruption shipped (4 days red on CI, invisible locally).** The self-host's emitted C contains constructs that Linux/gcc accepts as WARNINGS but Apple clang (the dev toolchain) rejects as ERRORS, so `self_host_runtime` + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` CC-FAIL on ~925/~36/8 fixtures locally for reasons UNRELATED to any given change → the dev never sees real runtime regressions; CI (Linux/gcc) is the only place these fixtures execute. **Workaround proven this round:** a PATH-shimmed `cc` wrapping `/usr/bin/cc -Wno-int-conversion -Wno-implicit-function-declaration -Wno-incompatible-pointer-types -Wno-return-mismatch -Wno-return-type` makes the net regress down to 8 genuinely-platform-specific fixtures (the `send`/`clone_inplace`/`RxNode` macOS-header/fwd-decl clashes + `meta_platform_guard` OS-detection). **Fix options (own decision):** (a) make the test harness append these `-Wno-*` flags to its `cc` invocations on macOS (`tests/integration.rs` `self_host_emit_cc_run` :18426 + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` :18633) so local validation is faithful; OR (b) document the shim in CLAUDE.md Build&Test as the macOS prerequisite for self-host gates. Without one of these, every self-host codegen change is un-validatable on the dev box and ships blind to CI.
  - **🐛 CLEAN UP the gcc-tolerated-but-clang-rejected self-host EMIT classes (latent UB, benign on the gcc/x86_64 production target but real codegen sloppiness).** Surfaced as the ~36 CC-FAIL classes when the net runs under strict clang: (i) **int↔pointer untyped-local stores** — the self-host emits `void* local = (int32_t)0` for some unresolved-type locals (dead stores in `test_result_advanced`, but the class is real: a local whose type the lowerer couldn't resolve defaults to `void*` + an int-0 init); (ii) **non-void functions that fall off the end without a `return`** (`-Wreturn-mismatch`: `Column__col_len`, `IoError__display`, `MapIter__…__next`, `Color__eq`, etc. — the self-host emits a non-void signature but no terminal return on some path); (iii) **conflicting forward-decl vs definition types** (`X__clone_inplace`, `RxNode`) and the **`send` libc-symbol collision** (the self-host emits a function named `send`, clashing with macOS `<sys/socket.h>`; rename-mangle runtime-colliding symbols). All round-trip harmlessly on 64-bit gcc but are principled bugs; (i) is also the reason the `map` crash was a SEGFAULT rather than a clean error on x86_64. Own scout per class; lower priority than parity work but they erode the "reference-grade emit" bar.

Confirmed PRE-EXISTING on the baseline driver (String temp default, 9-byte leak), INDEPENDENT of the `e81233ac` clone fix (which clones for the borrowing wrapper). Root: the move-eligible temp is marked moved-into-the-call (CoW last-use), but the get_or wrapper takes the default BY BORROW + only clones it on miss → on a hit the temp is never consumed/dropped. Fix needs the call-site move/borrow decision reconciled with the borrowing-wrapper ABI (drop the temp at the call, or pass-by-value-and-let-wrapper-drop). Check both compilers. Own scout.

- **🆕 ROUND-11 INC-1 FOLLOW-UPS (async `spawn`/`await` cluster — remaining work after the Inc-1 base spawn/await wiring for named fns with int+void returns + scalar by-value params; see DONE.md for the Inc-1 entry):**

Inc-1 does NOT need this (all 3 Inc-1 fixtures await every Task, and `__gorget_await_X` does the join+free), so the move-zero in the EAwait arm is defensive-only. **⚠ Core #8 SHARED gap, NOT a self-host-vs-Rust divergence** (corrected by the Inc-1 output-review `ad2cd189`, which emitted Rust's own `--emit-c-lir`): **Rust gg ALSO does NOT free an un-awaited Task** — it DEFINES `Task__T__drop` but marks it `__attribute__((unused))` and never CALLs it for an un-awaited Task. Under LeakSanitizer BOTH binaries run clean (ctx stays reachable via the detached thread `pthread_create`+`pthread_detach`), so it's not LSan-detectable, and the self-host is at BEHAVIORAL PARITY with Rust (`spawn_join_on_drop` MATCHes). The reference-grade fix (honoring `spawn_join_on_drop`'s join-on-drop INTENT) = implement true RAII join-on-drop in BOTH backends (self-host: wire `drop_fn_for_type` → a Task drop that JOINs+frees at scope exit; Rust: actually CALL `Task__T__drop`). The allow-list DELIBERATELY excludes the rest: relaxing the gate to a bare `>= PRIM_COUNT` is a MEASURED −14 net loss (opaque/sync/refcounted params `AtomicInt`/`Mutex__int64_t`/`Shared`/`Channel` emit `unknown type name`). Inc-2b = the remaining branches the Rust C-emit (`emit_types.rs:418-440`) carries that Inc-2a does not: `ref_param_indices` (the `&__ctx->__name` mut-ref branch), the per-param `is_ptr` vs by-value resolution for String (Inc-2a passes String uniformly by-ptr, matching Rust's `default to Ptr if unknown`), `is_opaque`→`void*`, struct/enum returns, and the `clone_params` (Channel/Shared/Weak refcount-clone) path. - **🎨 CAST-VIA-CONSTRUCTION LANGUAGE REDESIGN (owner-approved DESIGN 2026-06-20; impl deferred) — RFC `docs/plans/cast-via-construction.md`. ⚠ §7+§8 are the AUTHORITATIVE resolution layer (supersede §3–§6); read those, not the old §3–§4 sketch.** Retire `as`, `Into`, `TryFrom`, `TryInto`: conversion IS construction. `T(x)` is the sole spelling; the `From` registry survives UNDER THE HOOD (a 1-arg ctor `Self(T)` auto-registers it — `From` = the capability name + the generic-bound name, §7.4 Seam B). Fallibility = the `throws` effect, TYPE-level (widening ctor = non-`throws`, narrowing = `throws`) — NO value-flow analysis. **RESOLVED 2026-06-20 (§7.3):** (1) narrowing **overflow → THROW** (recoverable; off the strict-panic stance) — arithmetic overflow is a FAULT not a contract error, see error-model doc; (2) **CORRECTED 2026-06-22:** flavors + float→int modes are **NAMED-ARG CONSTRUCTORS** (`byte(clamping=x)`/`int(truncating=f)`/`int(rounding=f)`) — type=callee, mode=static label (Swift's `UInt8(clamping:)`); the 2026-06-20 source-method detour (`x.clamped(byte)`) was REVERSED (it conflated a static arg-LABEL with a runtime mode-PARAM AND put a type in value-arg position); (3) value→String is **`String(T)`** (= `Displayable.display()`, infallible), `str(x)` stays rejected. **Seams (§7.4):** float→int has no bare `int(f)` form (mode-labeled construction `int(rounding=f)` etc.); generics relocate the From/TryFrom distinction to bound-form (not deleted). **§8:** 9-language survey — Into/Try* dissolve (Into = From-backwards; TryFrom only exists because Rust's From can't fail — a `throws` ctor IS the "Try"); no silent-lossy default (5-language lesson); the `throws` annotation = C#'s implicit/explicit (no new keyword). Remaining work = implementation only; both-compilers + spec + book + negative-fixtures, staged per §6; the EAs `as` fix (parity #1) built the reusable `GICast`→LIR machinery. Own scout→brief→reviews when funded.
- **🧭 ERROR-MODEL LANGUAGE DIRECTION (owner brainstorm 2026-06-20; DRAFT — SCOUT + 3 SEQUENTIAL REVIEWS DONE, clean SIGN OFF `999a54a1`; several owner decisions folded) — `docs/plans/error-model.md`.** Spawned by the cast RFC's arithmetic-overflow sub-decision. Model: value channel + a typed error channel; error TYPES classified **contract** (parse/IO/conversion — mandatory-handle, on the API surface) vs **fault** (overflow/bounds/div0/OOM). Core argument = the IMPOSSIBILITY (§3): recoverable-default-overflow + informative-row + universal-typed = pick two → faults MUST be a non-contract kind. **OWNER DECISIONS (2026-06-21):** (1) **§0.5 reversal ACCEPTED** but ADDITIVE — overflow STAYS panic-by-default (safety rationale intact for the uncaught case); we ADD opt-in recovery, so docs get "panics by default AND catchable," not a full overturn. (2) **Q14 RESOLVED** — faults = a structured closed **`Fault` enum** (never String), the "typed/untyped" framing was a conflation of value-repr (A) / in-signature (B) / static-exhaustive-catch (C); decided A=enum, B=out-of-signature, C=dynamic-match. (3) **§9.1 phased (BOTH, PHASED)** — TYPING vs PROPAGATION are orthogonal; **PHASE 1** = `Fault equip Error` (unified `catch` for both kinds) + panic-by-default + **LOCAL-only catch** (`(a*b) catch Overflow: …` lowers to a checked-op-branch-to-handler = the existing `--overflow=checked` op, no unwind, no ubiquity) + exhaustiveness-via-implicit-panic-default; **⭐ Phase 1 sidesteps EVERY unwind-dependent blocker** (B2 greenfield-unwind, Q9 drop-across-unwind, Q15 FFI, Q16 main-boundary, §3.1 partial-state — all PHASE 2). **PHASE 2** = deep/boundary (server-keeps-serving) catch, needs the greenfield unwind infra, strictly additive over Phase 1. Precedents: Zig/Koka/Swift-6/Midori/Erlang/Java-checked (all accurate per scout). **Open (§9 Q1–Q16 + §9.1):** the fast knob (debug-checked/release-wrap), `meta`-overflow three-way split (wraps silently today `meta.rs:1278-1280`), `Never` spelling, Result reconciliation, full fault set, self-host parity. **§1/§4 "one channel/two kinds" framing KEPT (owner-decided 2026-06-21, "minimal clarify"); a §4 clarification + neutralized-forward-notes landed `4a785ef8`.** Touches the WHOLE language — Phase 1 is the small, shippable increment; the self-host (wall-to-wall arithmetic) needs a parity story first. **PHASE-1 SPEC (§11) REVIEWED 2026-06-21: 4 fresh SEQUENTIAL passes → clean SIGN OFF (`29f6357b`); briefable.** Central impl item = a **NEW LIR checked-faultable-op-with-handler-branch shape in BOTH backends** (no existing trap to re-point — all checks trap inline at C/LLVM emit, after drop passes; model the branch as GIR/LIR CFG like `lower_catch_expr` so drops stay correct). Phase-1 faults = Overflow+DivByZero (inline) + Bounds via `gorget_array_safe_get`+NULL-branch (brief decides Phase 1 vs 1.5); OOM→Phase 2. Also: `Fault equip Error` (3 synth methods display/debug/source), closed-`Fault` scrutinee + panic-default match rule, catch-on-non-throwing = NEW AST/grammar/typecheck form (don't perturb contract `catch`), per-expr overflow-mode override, handler-bb constructs the `Fault` variant. **FINAL pre-impl gate PASSED 2026-06-21 (`a9b212c7`): plan+code review + self-host scout + a confirming pass → clean SIGN OFF, IMPLEMENTATION-READY for both backends.** Final review caught + fixed a stale contradiction (§3.1/§9 Q1 asserted blanket "never inline" — now carved out: boundary-only = Phase-2 deep; Phase-1 local catch is safe, branch-before-store). **SELF-HOST (§11.8, scout-verified): NO prerequisites to fix — the key dependency is already satisfied (self-host emits CHECKED arithmetic today, per-instruction overflow field, `lower_catch_expr` CFG template, enum/trait construction all present); Rust-first regresses NO self-host gate (own source keeps panic-default); self-host fast-follows with the SAME new LIR shape + a greenfield fault-catch AST/typecheck path (`infer.gg` has no ECatch handler) + the `map_binop` unknown-op→OP_ADD footgun to guard.** Sequencing (§11.7): shared LIR shape FIRST → one backend → typecheck → second backend → fixtures; the overflow-mode override is ADDITIVE (don't mutate the `overflow_wrap` global). **ERROR-MODEL — SUPERSEDED BY DECISION BATCH 5 (2026-07-11 ledger LOG): the fault-catch frontier is CLOSED** — D25 removes fault-catch entirely (self-host Inc2, `Fault equip Error`, Phase-2 deep/boundary catch all cancelled; deep catch rejected on the merits, boundary containment = D24's Task-join `TaskFault`, OOM rides D24/phase-3; the fault-participation machinery is deleted by the Batch-C2 track). **SURVIVING open §9 Qs (unaffected by the fault redesign, each still needs an owner ruling eventually):** the fast knob (debug-checked/release-wrap), `meta`-overflow split (`meta.rs:1278` — partly covered by D18), `Never` spelling, Result reconciliation.

(4) cc is LINEAR in C size (no super-linearity); emitted-C expansion ratio grew only 17.3×→19.3× (+22%/fn from drop-elab/lazy-iter/FaultCheck/clone-insertion = real work, NOT a mono explosion). **ACTIONABLE LEVERS (owner-decision; none are regressions to fix, they're speedups):** (a) **run the heavy C self-host tests under LLVM / drop from the default C sweep → ~250–300s saving, zero compiler work** (the self-host serial cluster = 632s/74% of the 859s; LLVM already avoids the 5× 27MB-cc, hence LLVM 540–587s vs C 859s) — tradeoff: C-backend bootstrap leaves the default C sweep; (b) build bootstrap stages with `cc -O1` not `-O0` (~40–65s; probe first); (c) continue clone-elimination (10.3M clones/517MB still). **Do NOT pursue:** overflow gate (0.17%), a mono-explosion fix (none exists), TU-split for per-byte cc (linear), lowering `MAX_GEN` (already breaks at convergence).

**PARALLELIZATION MODEL (owner-updated 2026-06-15 — SUPERSEDES old "1 parity chain only"):** run as many well-scoped tracks CONCURRENTLY as you have work for, in PARALLEL worktrees, EVEN IF they touch the SAME files (worktree isolation + orchestrator-merge-at-integrate makes file overlap safe). **PARITY tracks PARALLELIZE too** (the old "wins serialize on the lower*.gg cluster" predated the lowerer split + ignores wins in `traits.gg`/`infer.gg`/`lir_lower.gg`/`lir_codegen.gg`). **ONLY constraint: do NOT parallelize a track that is REALLY BROAD** (sprawls the whole lowerer cluster, e.g. method-generic-mono); run that alone. (1-PERF/1-CLEANUP/1-DOC alongside is a useful MINIMUM, not a cap.) Narrower "sequence X" notes elsewhere are superseded EXCEPT where genuinely broad.

- **🐛 [HIGH — LEAK, BOTH BACKENDS — A2-R2 M1 scout/executor 2026-07-12; ORTHOGONAL to the ICE fix] Operator-overload resource-ARGUMENT temp is never drop-registered → leak.** When the RHS operand of a user operator overload is a heap-owning resource TEMP, the borrowed arg passed to `add`/`sub`/… is not drop-registered after the call and leaks (~64B). Reproduces on PLAIN binary `+`, NO compound assign, untouched code: `Acc c = a + mk(5)` where `Acc{int total; Vector[int] buf}` leaks the temp's `buf` (LSan). Also `a += mk(5)` (identifier compound, `assigns.rs` identifier arm) leaks, and `v[0] += Acc(5, one(5))` (index compound with an INLINE-ctor RHS) leaks — all the SAME class (the RHS operand temp, not the element). Root = operator-overload arg drop-registration, a different subsystem than the compound-assign lowering. This is why the A2-R2 M1 fixture uses a CALL-shaped RHS (`v[0] += mk(5)`) — which IS drop-registered on the index arm and ASan-clean — so the ICE-fix gate is honest without tripping this bug. Repro: build `Acc c = a + mk(5)` with `--sanitize`. Own scout; wire a plain-`+` ASan regression fixture.
- **🐛💥 [HIGH — ICE, BOTH BACKENDS — A2-R2 M1 scout/executor 2026-07-12; Core-#4 sibling of the closed vector/dict ICE] Custom-`Index`-equipped type with a RESOURCE element + `add` overload still ICEs on `custom[i] += x`.** A2-R2 M1 scoped the borrow-in-place fix to Vector/Dict (matching the filed bug); a custom container equipping `Index[K, Acc]` whose `get` returns an OWNED resource element hits `cur_is_borrow=false` in `lower_compound_assign` (`assigns.rs`, the custom-`__get` branch) → reaches the shallow-copy path `builder.assign(cur_local, cur_val)` → the SAME resource-move validator ICE at `src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:1763` ("shallow copy of resource"). REACHABLE, not typecheck-rejected (probe passes `gg check`, panics at `gg build`). Repro shape: `struct Acc{int total}` (custom Drop + `Add`), `struct Box2{Acc a; Acc b}` equipping `Index[int,Acc]`/`IndexMut[int,Acc]` (get returns `self.a.clone()`, set uses `!value`), then `bx[0] += mk(5)`. Fix: the custom `get` returns an owned value by its trait signature, so borrow-in-place isn't a drop-in — needs a move-out/move-back or a borrow-returning custom-index path. Own scout. (An R2 NOTE breadcrumb marks the site in `assigns.rs`.)
- **[LOW — A2-R2 M2 polish 2026-07-12] `E_MoveWithoutOperator` field/index sub-place message renders the ROOT name, not the exact sub-place.** `tainted_place_name` (`src/semantic/safety/helpers.rs`) uses `find_root_def_id`, which returns only the ROOT binding's name — so a field/index sub-place (`hh.r`, `v[0]`) renders `hh.clone()` / `v.clone()` instead of `hh.r.clone()` / `v[0].clone()`. The A2-R2 M2 message sidesteps the inaccuracy ("copy the sub-place with `hh.clone()` … a bare `!` on a field/index sub-place is a partial move"), but the exact sub-place text is nicer. Fix = thread the place SPAN through `tainted_place_name` and slice the source (or have a place-printer walk the identifier/self/field/tuple/index chain). Not required by the capture-no-`!` GATE. Own scout.
- **🐛 [BACKEND DIVERGENCE — resources decision scout 2026-07-06] LLVM does not honor `alloc=` into a bare Arena the way C does.** The bare-allocator leak (q2a2) and the `.destroy()`-then-use UAF (q2b2) reproduce on C ASan but NOT on LLVM — LLVM appears not to route `Vector/String(alloc=a)` into the arena at all. Backends must be at parity (whichever semantics the A10 ruling picks). Probes: /tmp/recover_scout_resources/.

- **🐛 [matcluster #2 RE-SCOPED → own track, filed 2026-07-06] Loop-carried bare-param materialize needs a whole-function pre-loop analysis.** `deadwrite_ok_loop_read_before_write` prints 1,1,1,1; ggdef-adjudicated expected = 1,2,3,1 (NO run expectation is committed — the wrong output is NOT locked in; the fixture keeps only its check_gg_silent_for). Measured stop-condition (matcluster executor mini-prototype, both backends): extending the `cow_lazy_mat_flag` substrate at the `is_bare_param` branch (context.rs:3319 at time of filing) fails the entry-binding conflict — cc `incompatible types 'GorgetArray' from 'void *'`, llc `'%p0' defined with type 'ptr' but expected '%GorgetArray'` — because the lazy substrate's value-slot+flag are created at a PRE-LOOP bind site (`emit_lazy_loopcarried_borrow`, VarDecl-triggered) and a bare param has no pre-loop bind; retyping the param slot mid-loop retro-types the `void* __v0 = (void*)__p0` entry binding (the same failure class matcluster #3 fixed for the non-loop rebind). Fix shape: detect bare-param-mutated-in-loop up front and bind a FRESH owned local at the loop pre-header (mirror matcluster #3 — do NOT retype the param slot in place), kept stable across per-iteration `restore_locals`, so the loop body reads/writes the persistent private copy. Self-host classified SAME-BUG by the matcluster scout (both-compiler fix + bootstrap gate) — re-confirm and mirror. Re-verify the gap: `cargo run -p ggdef -- run tests/fixtures/deadwrite_ok_loop_read_before_write.gg` (1,2,3,1 normative) vs `gg build`+run (1,1,1,1). **Also wire an `#[ignore]`d fixture asserting the correct 1,2,3,1** (per "Don't redesign around compiler gaps" — the gap currently has no fixture guarding the intended output). Own scout→brief→≥3 reviews. Evidence: /tmp/matx_p3/ (failing loop2_C.c / loop2_LLVM.ll).

### 🆕 ROUND-38 FOLLOW-UPS + DISCOVERED RESIDUALS (filed 2026-07-04; T-B landed = self-host `&self` MUTATION-INFERENCE pass — `compute_method_mutates_self` classifies each non-generic equip method read-only-vs-writes-self via a monotone fixpoint over self-callee edges, and the named-receiver CoW gate materializes ONLY genuine mutators. Both the SCAN (`mutinf_scan_expr`) and the lower_expr GATE are USER→BUILTIN order (Core #4), so a user `&self`-mutator NAMED like a read-only builtin (`get`/`map`/`peek`/…) is classified correctly for BOTH a RESOLVABLE named receiver AND a RESOLVABLE projected receiver (`v[i].get()` / `s.v[i].get()` / `o.inner.get()`) — the GATE's name-collision guard resolves the projected element/field type via `mutinf_recv_type_name` (the typed local slot + `index_value_type_name` + `GirTypeInfo.fields`) and reads the PRECISE `method_mutates_self` map (bomb-safe: a genuine projected builtin `v[i].len()` resolves to `Elem__len`, absent → no clone). Closes the R37 T1 named-user-`&self`-mutator write-through divergence for NON-GENERIC equips [see the ROUND-37 item below]; +5 MATCH fixtures; measured peak self-compile RSS ~625-628 MB == BASE, self-compile wall-time ~128.9s == BASE (NOT the 14GB bomb). New `gir.gg` field `method_mutates_self`; `lower.gg` mutinf_* walker+fixpoint + projected-type resolver + two arm-count lints; `lower_expr.gg` gate wiring + name-collision guard (named + projected).)
- **[HIGH — self-host latent FOOTGUN; from T-B] `apply_collect_target_rewrites` (`lower.gg:2665`) RESETS equip-method `self` param `.ownership` to bare (0) in `m.items`** — the AST reconstruction silently drops the `&self`/`!self` ownership axis on the reconstructed param, so `mi_meth.params.get(0).ownership` is UNRELIABLE for the self-convention (T-B works around it by reading the typed `fn_borrow_params[key][0]` populated by the earlier pre-pass). Any future pass that trusts `m.items` equip-method param ownership will silently mis-classify self. Fix = preserve the param ownership through the reconstruction (or re-derive it from the typed maps at the reconstruction site). Latent beyond T-B's scope.
- **[MED — self-host, Rust; T-B residual, Core #8] GENERIC-equip `&self` mutator on a bare-value-param named receiver still WRITES THROUGH.** `compute_method_mutates_self` classifies NON-generic equips only (`type_params.len()==0`), so `Cell__assign` (a generic-equip method) is absent from `method_mutates_self`; the named-receiver gate returns false → self-host `Y/Y` vs Rust `Y/A`. DEGRADE-TO-BASE (no NEW miscompile — the pre-R38 self-host wrote through for ALL named user-receiver mutators; T-B closed the non-generic sub-case; `cow_materialize_projected_root` is itself double-gated on a bare-value root so a false read-only cannot corrupt). Executable breadcrumb: `tests/fixtures/known_gaps/generic_equip_mutator_named_recv.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d `cow_named_recv_generic_equip_gap` integration test (asserts the intended Y/A; kept in a subdir so it stays OUT of the runtime_diff corpus). Fix = classify generic-equip methods too, mirroring the `fn_sigs` generic-instances pre-pass, then RE-MEASURE RSS (more-mutating → re-bomb risk — the double-gate limits it to bare-param roots, but confirm).
- **[LOW — self-host, Rust; T-B residual, Core #8] name-collision on an UNRESOLVABLE projection stays read-only (both the SCAN and the GATE tail).** The user→builtin order closes the name-collision for a RESOLVABLE receiver (named, or a projection whose root local + field/element types resolve — verified: `v[i].get()` / `s.v[i].get()` materialize). Two unresolvable tails remain, both DEGRADE-TO-BASE (write-through, no bomb): (a) SCAN — in `mutinf_scan_expr`, a self-projection whose type resolves to −1 at scan time (`self.field.get()` where `self.field`→−1) falls to `builtin(get)=Some(false)`; (b) GATE — in `mutinf_recv_type_name`, a projected receiver whose base is NOT a named-root chain (e.g. an element of a METHOD-returned collection `f().v[0].get()`, or a root local absent from `ctx.locals`) resolves to "" → the guard returns false. Fix = a fuller projection-type oracle (or route the scan/gate through the typechecker's per-link `expr_link_types` where present), re-validating RSS since a conservative "unresolved ⇒ mutating" fallback here is the re-bomb the whole pass avoids.
- **[LOW — self-host, Rust; T-B residual] self reborrowed into a local then mutated is not traced by the scan.** `Holder r = self; r.set_name(..)` inside a `&self` method — the scan does not recognise `r` as self-rooted (`mutinf_expr_is_self_rooted("r")` is false), so `r.set_name` is not attributed to self. VERIFIED HARMLESS in the self-host's CURRENT model: a bare `Holder r = self` bind COPIES (r is an independent value — `r.set_name` mutates the copy, not self), so the enclosing method is GENUINELY non-self-mutating → read-only classification is CORRECT, no divergence. The residual only bites IF a future `&`-reborrow-into-local (`auto v = &self.field; v.push(..)`, an aliasing bind that writes through) lands (the `&`-bind write-through itself landed round-38 — T-D flipped `cow_amp_bind_ref`/`cow_amp_bind_ref_field` to MATCH; its scalar-field/element sub-case is the deferred-capability residual filed below); then this path must default to MUTATING (belt-and-braces: the EMutableBorrow arm already flags a direct `&self`/`&self.field` formation as mutating). ⚠ SEPARATE DISCOVERED BUG (Rust, PRE-EXISTING, orthogonal to T-B): `Holder r = self; r.set_name(..)` inside a `&self` method makes **Rust gg PANIC** (`src/main.rs:2434` "gg main thread panicked"); the self-host compiles it and prints the read-only-correct result. Own scout (Rust `&self`→owned-local bind path).
- **[LOW — self-host; T-D adv(c) deferred capability, NOT a regression] scalar/struct-field & element `&`-bind write-through is VALUE-COPIED, not aliased.** T-D's `auto r = &b` / `&b.field` write-through fix (landed, `cow_amp_bind_ref`/`cow_amp_bind_ref_field` MATCH) GATES its bare branch on `inner == EIdentifier`, so a projection of a SCALAR/non-resource field or element (`auto r = &b.scalar_field` / `&b[scalar_i]`) routes to the SAFE value-bind fall-through (a copy) rather than a write-through mut-ptr. This is a DEFERRED CAPABILITY, not a regression (pre-fix it also cloned) — zero corpus occurrences today. Implementing the Ptr write-through for scalar-field/element `&`-binds (resolve the field/element slot address for value-typed fields) is the follow-up. Reference: `lower_stmt.gg` SVarDecl EMutableBorrow intercept.

- **✨ FEATURE — support forward / cross-module const-refs in a `const` initializer (fixpoint registration; Low)** (filed by the const-foldability round, 2026-06-25, DONE `316d818a`). A `const A = B + 1` where `B` is a const declared LATER (or imported, or a meta-const referenced from a `meta if` elif/else arm — the Track-9 re-review confirmed these ALL fold to 0 pre-fix, a pre-existing lowering gap now surfaced consistently at check time) currently REJECTS ("not a compile-time constant") because the lowering const-scan (`src/ir/lowering/mod.rs:546`) AND the matching typecheck pre-pass (`check_module_const_foldability`) fold in a SINGLE source-order pass — `B` isn't in `module_constants` when `A` is folded. Rejecting is correct-for-now (it used to silently miscompile to 0; reject is strictly better + the typecheck/lowering agree). To SUPPORT it: make the const-fold loop a FIXPOINT (iterate until no new const folds) in BOTH the lowering scan and the typecheck pre-pass (keep them mirrored — one source of truth). Then `const_forward_ref_error.gg` flips from reject to `11`; update it + its test. Pure ergonomics; no correctness gap today.

- **🐛 RUST-GG CODEGEN — `rethrow (String e): <String-transform>` LEAKS the transformed error String (LeakSanitizer)** (DISCOVERED 2026-06-22 by the throws-codegen-fix output-review's ASan sweep; PRE-EXISTING — reproduces IDENTICALLY on the pristine `717289b4` tree, NOT introduced by the throws-codegen fix). Repro under `--sanitize`: `tests/fixtures/rethrow_catch_binding.gg` (`int caller_rethrow(x): int v = helper_str(x) rethrow (String e): Err(f"transformed: {e}")` → `==…==ERROR: LeakSanitizer: 18 byte(s) leaked in 1 allocation(s)`) AND `tests/fixtures/error_rethrow_transform.gg` (`int port = try_parse(s) rethrow (String e): f"port error: {e}"` → leak through `gorget_string_format`). The freshly-formatted/allocated transform String (the new error payload built in the rethrow arm) is never freed on the error path. ⚠ ONLY the `rethrow`-with-a-String-transform shape leaks — `rethrow_basic`/`rethrow_bare` (no String-build transform) are CLEAN, and the entire 68-fixture throws/error ASan sweep is otherwise leak-free. Likely an ownership-register gap on the rethrow-arm transform result (the new Error payload's String isn't tracked for drop on the rethrow propagation path). Own scout (RUN under ASan, trace the unfreed alloc — `gorget_string_format` at the rethrow arm) → brief → fix at the rethrow-transform OWNERSHIP-register write site (register the transform-result String for drop / move it into the propagated Error). Low-pri (leak, not UB), but it is a real defect on BOTH the reference and self-host shape. NOT a quick guess.

- **🐛 SELF-HOST — `unwrap()`/`unwrap_error()` on a `Some(String)`/`Ok(String)`/`Error(String)` payload LEAKS ~6B (ASan; Rust gg CLEAN) — the Ok-side mirror of the `unwrap_error` fix `3d44495b`** (filed by the unwrap_error output-review, 2026-06-25). `Result.unwrap()` / `Option.unwrap()` on a resource (String) payload leaks under `--sanitize` through `gorget_string_clone_to_owned ← main` — a self-host↔Rust-gg drop-correctness divergence (Rust gg's `--sanitize` is clean). Reproduces with `.unwrap()` ALONE (no `unwrap_error`). `3d44495b` fixed the clone-on-borrow for the Err side (`unwrap_error`); the Ok side (`unwrap`) has the same borrowing-getter shape but its cloned payload isn't tracked for drop. Own scout (RUN under ASan, trace the `clone_to_owned` at the `unwrap` consume site) → fix the self-host `__option_unwrap`/`__result_unwrap` codegen to register/drop the cloned payload (mirror the Err-side fix). Low-pri (leak, not UB).

- **🐛 GG_IMPL self-host `exec` is shell-based (`system()` → `sh -c`) — word-splits on spaces/shell-metacharacters in paths** (filed by the run-mode output-review `afd371e2`, 2026-06-22; PRE-EXISTING, NOT introduced by the run-mode fix `d2e64d22`). The self-host driver's `exec` (`std.process`, runtime `gorget_exec` → `process_runtime.c:5-6` `system(cmd)`) is used for BOTH the run path (`driver.gg:704`, now `path_absolute`-resolved) AND the `cc_cmd` (`driver.gg:695-696`) — so a build/run path or `-o` target containing a space or shell metacharacter word-splits or injects. Rust gg avoids this entirely by exec'ing the built binary shell-free via `Command::new(&exe_path).status()` (`src/main.rs:2489-2497`) and building the cc argv as a vector. Reference-grade fix = give the self-host a shell-free exec (argv vector, no `sh -c`) for both sites. Lower-pri (no corpus path has spaces today); doesn't trip Core-#8 (pre-existing, unchanged). Own small scout→fix. (Cosmetic sibling: `tests/integration.rs:~16292` `self_host_cli_pipeline` step-6 comment says "NOT where the .gg lives" but the cwd IS where it lives — the bite is the bare `-o` name; fix the comment on contact.)

- **🧵 SELF-HOST SYNC-PRIMITIVE remaining (the 4 originals + local-ctor + Guard-field-access are in DONE.md). NEW (non-blocking):**
  - **(c) Mutex/RWLock LOCK-OBJECT leak — Core-#8 ≥2-bug, multi-step track. Semantics are NOT an open question (DOC-SETTLED 2026-06-19); the work is impl-vs-spec drift + a missing leak gate.** Scouts `a740cceeb` (bare) + this session's docs-grounding + RUN-measurement.
    - **SEMANTICS ARE SETTLED — a lock IS a Resource (do NOT re-open as an "owner decision"; my earlier framing was over-cautious):** `language-design.md:319` (Resource = "owns a heap allocation, file handle, **or lock** — cannot be implicitly copied"); `devbook/18-runtime-abi.md:84-99` (Mutex is an opaque handle COVERING a runtime-owned allocation — "the runtime owns both halves"); `book/14-concurrency.md:135` + `book/16-smart-pointers.md:75` (the ARC owns the Mutex, "dropped when the last owner goes away"); `language-reference.md:3589` (guards drop to release the lock). The runtime ships a `gorget_mutex_new`/`gorget_mutex_free` PAIR and never calls free. The lone dissenter is the type metadata (`builtins.rs:587-588` `MUTEX = CopySemantics::Trivial, drop_fn: None`; self-host mirrors via `GtPtr(Mutex__int64_t)` + `is_droppable_type(GtPtr)=false` `lower_drops.gg:160-163`). The double-free worry is INVERTED: Resource = no-implicit-copy → single owner → single drop, so the Resource classification PREVENTS the double-free.
    - **(a) BOTH compilers leak, across BOTH forms (RUN-measured):** bare `Mutex[int]` (`mutex_basic`) leaks 136B; the dominant **`Shared[Mutex[T]]`** form (`shared_float`, via the `shared`-keyword CFA promotion) leaks **176B / 4 allocations in Rust gg** (trace `Shared__Mutex__double__new → main`). So this is not a bare-Mutex corner — the ARC-wrapped canonical form leaks too.
    - **(b) The fix is the ARC-CONTENT-DEEP-DROP branch, NOT a Mutex flag-flip:** `shared_float`'s 4 leaked allocations (ARC control block + Mutex + boxed value + …) show `Shared`'s drop-at-refcount-0 does NOT dispatch its inner type's `drop_fn` today — a broad `Shared[anything-droppable]` hole. The fix must teach the `Shared` content-drop path to deep-drop its inner (which then frees Mutex AND any droppable inner uniformly), in BOTH compilers, alongside flipping `MUTEX`→`Resource`+`gorget_mutex_free` (and the self-host `GtPtr`→droppable + `drop_fn_for_type` Mutex/RWLock arms + `GIDropIfAlive` ptr-slot load). Confirm there's no path where a bare Mutex handle is a NON-owning view (`devbook/18:98` notes some opaque handles ARE non-owning — but `gorget_mutex_new` always-allocates says Mutex is owning; RUN-confirm).
    - **(c) PREREQUISITE — build the leak gate FIRST (the fix is unverifiable without it):** `self_host_runtime` snapshots check STDOUT only → leak-blind (same structural blind spot as the DIAG-blind `*_comparison` harness). These fixtures PASS today while leaking. Before fixing, add ASan-checked leak fixtures for: the bare `Mutex[int]` form, the implicit `shared`-keyword form (`shared_float`-shape), AND the EXPLICIT `Shared[Mutex[int]](Mutex[int](0))` constructor form (`language-design.md:1634` — has ZERO coverage today). Without the gate, a "fix" is trust-me (exactly the trap `05a40cbf` fell into). ASan-class, parity-neutral, multi-step. Own scout→brief→≥3 reviews→execute.

The zeroing is half-right (a closure may run after/outside the block); conservative-correct fix direction: keep the zeroing for the body walk but gate ESCAPING mut-captures of outer vars at closure-definition-inside-arena, or track "closure defined under arena_depth>0" and apply the producer at its call sites inside the block. NOT covered by the landed core (which gates statement/expr positions only). Probe `p18_closure_escape.gg` in `/tmp/recover_r33_arenaescape_probes.tar.gz`.
  - **🐛 NEW (same scout) N1 sub-hole (b): nested-arena depth-blindness (LOGICAL, ASan-INVISIBLE).** `arena_scoped_vars` is a flat set — no depth tag — so inner-arena→outer-arena-var escapes (`vo = !vi` with `vo` under the OUTER `with Arena`, `vi` under the INNER) skip every gate (`target ∈ arena_scoped_vars`). Runtime happens to read valid bytes because the inner arena's storage is drawn FROM the outer arena (nesting rule) and bump free is a no-op — logically dangling, physically intact until the OUTER destroy, invisible to ASan without arena poisoning (probes `p17`/`p17b`: check-clean, run-CLEAN). Fix (follow-up to the landed N1 core): tag `arena_scoped_vars` values with their `arena_depth` and reject on depth mismatch (inner→outer), or poison arena regions on destroy under `--sanitize`.

  - Each group → its own brief→review→execute→gate round. **Next highest-leverage:** spawn-EXECUTION semantics (largest async follow-on); the INT-zero WRONG subset; the 61 next-layer async CC-FAILs.

Gate full integration + `fixed_point`.

- **🧩 CoW MATERIALIZATION — durable reference (THE RULE + GROUND TRUTH).**
  - **THE RULE (owner-decided):** *Never implicitly clone except when absolutely necessary for safety.* Order **borrow > move > clone**. An implicit clone is legitimate ONLY when BOTH: (1) the destination must OWN (a real ownership boundary), AND (2) the source can be neither aliased nor moved (lives past this point, OR is a collection element / `.get()` result / view-method result / parameter). Any clone NOT forced by (1)∧(2) is a bug. Explicit-type bindings BORROW (identical to `auto`). For collection-element/view/param sources, ownership-taking is clone-NOT-move. (= the `CLAUDE.md` "Ownership at Consuming Positions" contract.)
  - **GROUND TRUTH (re-cite before acting):** Enforcement = `validate_consume_sites` (`src/ir/validate.rs:2400`) runs UNCONDITIONALLY in `lower_module` (`mod.rs:1892`) and `panic!`s (`:1977`) on the first owning position receiving a borrow with no preceding clone/move ⇒ a MISSING materialization CRASHES, doesn't miscompile; ANY clone-removal must keep it GREEN. Taxonomy: `ImplicitCloneReason` (`src/ir/mod.rs:183`, 11 variants, 39 sites) via `ensure_owned_at_boundary` (`context.rs:1752`), `ensure_owned_at_consuming_arg` (`context.rs:1923`), `emit_enum_init_owned` (`context.rs:1243`) + write-side `cow_before_mutation` (`context.rs:2658`) + a GorgetString-ONLY runtime materialize net. Var-decl tree `lower_var_decl_assign_mode` (`stmts/mod.rs:1081-1273`): A/C→Borrow, F/G→Move, **B/D/E→Clone** (B = cross-type live `Str→GorgetString`; D = CoW-unsafe live; E = `View`→owned String). Clean references: `CLAUDE.md` + `docs/book/11-ownership.md:282-300` + `language-reference.md:2321`/`:1624`.
  - **Branch B is DEAD CODE — not an over-clone (empirically confirmed agent aa18fc8b).** Branch B (`stmts/mod.rs:1170-1179`) fired 0 times across 1121 fixtures — guard `!is_resource_type(rhs) && clone_fn_for_ptr(rhs).is_some()` is the EMPTY SET (every type with a registered clone fn carries `copy_semantics=Resource`). FOLLOW-UP (evidence-first): delete with a tombstone (after confirming no `GG_BACKEND=llvm`/non-fixture path reaches it), OR find the uncovered shape. Do NOT "convert B to Copy".
  - **Branch E over-clone (`stmts/mod.rs:1235-1241`) — RE-VERIFY IT FIRES FIRST.** Flagged (eager clone of a `View`-source same-type `String b = view`) but never confirmed firing. Instrument E + build the corpus to confirm a non-zero count + real over-clone BEFORE any fix. Defer-to-mutation infra exists (`set_view_of`/`views_of_source`/`cow_before_mutation` Case 4); higher risk (past naive view/alias removals regressed 50+ fixtures). Evidence-first.

- **(Medium, CORRECTNESS) Index-load `STATIC_VEC[i].field` on a static `Vector[struct]` returns a ZEROED struct.** `STATIC_VEC[i].field` reads `0`/empty while `STATIC_VEC.get(i).unwrap().field` reads correctly AND local `[i]` indexing works. A SEPARATE bug in the `IndexLoad` by-value-element-read path for *static* (global) struct vectors (index-load against a global slot produces a zeroed temp). Repro: `public static Vector[Item] T = [Item("a",1)]` + `print(T[0].name)` → empty. Likely backend codegen distinguishing global vs local collection base. `static_vec_literal.gg` sidesteps via `.get()`; flip the `#[ignore]`'d `static_vec_index_load` test when fixed.

- **(Low/Medium, CORRECTNESS) Static-decl initializers are NOT type-checked against their declared type.** `public static int X = [1,2,3]` is accepted by `gg check` while the LOCAL form is rejected. Mostly latent, BUT Bug-B Stage 1 changed its symptom for collection-literal RHS (`initializer_needs_synthetic_fn` keys on RHS shape → garbage pointer + C warning). Real fix = add static-decl initializer type-checking (mirror the local var-decl check).

- **🚨 (High, CORRECTNESS) Bug B STAGE 2 — remaining = #2 ONLY (`__gorget_box_clone` link marker); #1 variant-collision LANDED `5aab25d3`, see DONE.md + the EnumInit SSOT entry above.** Routing the Box-recursive `RUNTIME_FNS`/`RESOURCES` AST statics (`compiler/data/resources.gg:439,57`) through `lower_function` surfaces a chain of latent gaps; #1 (bare-variant-collision, the self-host parallel of Rust `847e767b`) is now closed on both compilers (RUN-verified 2026-06-24), leaving #2:
  - **#2 (stage-2 LINK) — `__gorget_box_clone` marker emitted as a literal call: NOT fixed.** With #1 fixed, stage-2 link fails (`undefined reference to __gorget_box_clone`). It's a Rust-side MARKER, not a real symbol — Rust `emit_types.rs:1164-1219` INTERCEPTS `clone_fn=="__gorget_box_clone"` + INLINES. Self-host `lir_codegen.gg` lacks the interception → emits a literal call. FIX = mirror Rust `emit_types.rs:1207-1219` (clone-fn mapping ~`lir_lower.gg:1344` `return "gorget_box_clone"`). Then re-run `fixed_point` (watch for #3+). **BuiltinMethodDecl port stays BLOCKED** until Stage 2 lands (held `81d654db` + patch).

- **(Low) Bootstrap stage-1 timeout default (120s) too short.** `GG_STAGE1_TIMEOUT_SECS` is blown by slow lower.gg/driver.gg/lir_lower.gg compiles (~100-500s) — bump to 900-1200. `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` does NOT honor this knob (hardcoded 600s/stage). **CI now sets it to 900 in `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (2026-06-26, after run 28223658994 tripped the 120s default on the slow x86_64 runner)**; the in-code default is still 120s — bump it (or auto-scale floor) if local/other CI hits it.

- **(Medium, LLVM, FLAKY?) `leak_string_heavy` fails under the LLVM backend on x86_64 CI** (run 28223658994, 2026-06-26): prints `p1..p5=0` (every per-pattern leak delta is zero) yet `leaked=true` — i.e. `p1==0 and ... and p5==0` evaluated false despite all operands being 0. C backend passes (aarch64 local + x86_64 CI); not reproducible locally (no `llc` installed). `src/` was UNCHANGED since the last green LLVM run (27923767349, 2026-06-22, where this test passed), so it is NOT introduced by the pushed self-host/docs commits. Contradiction points to an LLVM-backend uninitialized/nondeterministic read or a long `and`-chain/`not` miscompile (latent, likely intermittent). Rerun in progress to confirm flaky vs. deterministic. If deterministic, investigate LLVM lowering of chained `and` + `not` into the `bool all_zero` slot.

## High

- **Self-host parser: bare-return-type ownership sigil (`int ! foo()` / `String & bar()` OUTSIDE `[...]`).** The 2026-06-22 parser round consumed the trailing `!`/`&` sigil inside generic-arg brackets (`Vector[int !]` via `parse_type_with_ownership`/`skip_ownership_markers` — landed in self_host_typechecker + self_host_resolver; `self_host_parser` already had it). NOT covered: a bare ownership sigil on a top-level return type or param type written without brackets. No current fixture exercises this shape; if one is added, the self-host parser will derail the same way `Vector[int !]` did (sigil left unconsumed). Fix = teach the top-level `parse_type` / return-type path to consume the trailing sigil (mirror Rust `parse_type_with_ownership`, `src/parser/types.rs:20`). Add a fixture first.

- **Self-host snag #4: scalar slot for struct value via `EFieldAccess`-as-method return-type inference fallback.** `String x = path.byte_slice(...)` / `__gg_CsvTable t = parse_csv(...)`: `var_type` in `lower.gg`'s SVarDecl inherits the init expr's inferred type, which falls through to `I64_TYPE` when the call routes through `EFieldAccess`-as-method (~:2035) or `infer_method_return_type` doesn't know the return type → slot typed `int64_t`, downstream reads get zeros (manifests as `OutputMismatch`). Fix: (a) prefer the DECLARED type when concrete and inferred defaulted to I64; (b) OR fix the writer — the `EFieldAccess`-as-method path at `~:2035` returns `UNIT_TYPE` blindly instead of `infer_method_return_type`/`fn_sigs`. Affects `csv_delimiters.gg` + a tail binding `String last = path.byte_slice(...)`.

- **Branch-merging-expression helper (Cluster A, post-Snag-#39 audit). Defer.** Three sites use the discipline (`assign_match_arm_to_result` #31, `lower_catch_expr` #38, `build_if_chain_expr` #51 — sized from `expected_type`/first-non-divergent + routed through `assign_match_arm_to_result`). A unifying helper would carry awkward params (match arms iterate, if/elif has named slots, catch has the err-binding prelude). Reassess when a 4th instance appears or one drifts.

- **Safety-pass branch-divergence audit (Cluster C, post-Snag-#39 audit). Defer.** Snag #39's fix added `save_branch_state`/`restore_branch_state` around `Expr::Catch`/`Expr::Rethrow`. No other actively-leaking sites (`DefaultOp`/`And/Or`/`OptionalChain` checked clean — `self.diverged` is only set by `Return/Throw/Break/Continue`). If divergence tracking is extended to noreturn-call expressions (with `panic`/`exit` typed-Never work), audit these. Also consider upgrading Catch/Rethrow from save+restore to save+merge.

- **Self-host: extend `tc_types.expr_types` write-through to more Expr arms; consider span-keyed-sidecar replacement.** Rust writes `expr_types: FxHashMap<Span, TypeId>` at 10 sites (`typecheck.rs` 1619/1712/1756/1762/1770/1778/2026/2049/4481/4502); self-host covers the architectural-analogue subset (EAwait/ESpawn/parse written; 41+ infer_expr_type-family conversion + Rust 1770's inlined-builtin-chain deferred). Two follow-ons:
  1. **Extend write-through coverage** as the lowerer grows query sites (builtin-method chain, closure-return inference, EAs cast, EBlock body-ret). Add per query site, never speculatively (full-coverage cost ~100s on self-host's own source).
  2. **Span-keyed sidecar smell.** Both use a `Dict[Span, TypeId]` sidecar (byte offsets, span-collision-prone; today protected by per-module offset shifting in `loader.gg`). Cleaner = a typed field on `SpannedExpr` (`int resolved_type`). Pioneering this diverges from Rust — only worth it if Rust agrees to follow or a span-collision bug surfaces. Pair-port to Rust if/when redesigned.

- **Rust frontend: unify the 7 `lower_for_*` functions into a single scaffold + per-type element extractor** (`src/ir/lowering/stmts/for_loops.rs`, 1159 lines, ~80% duplicated scaffold). Self-host's `lower_loops.gg::lower_for` (`lower_for_vector` fast path + `lower_for_iterator` protocol) is the reference-grade shape. **Three layers** (ship in order, green tests gating each): (1) helper extraction (`alloc_for_blocks`/`emit_else_arm_tail`/`init_borrow_iter_local`, ~150 lines retired, no semantic change, ~half day); (2) filter abstraction for dict/set (`with_state_filter`, ~30 lines, ~a day total); (3) trait-based unification (`trait CollectionLowerer` + `lower_for_scaffold<L>` + 7 backends, ~300-400 lines, highest win + highest risk, 2-3 days total). Per-kind variability that doesn't unify: `lower_for_string` (source-aware ptr setup), `lower_for_range` (no iter_op), dict/set (state-check filter + output-param destructure), `lower_for_iterable` (no incr_bb). Verification: each layer ships independently with a full integration sweep (1122/1122). Tied-off after: the trait-equipped-methods-in-`fn_sigs` entry + the iter-chain `lowerer_comparison` mismatches.

- **Runtime-side panic locations (~50+ sites in `src/backend/c/c_runtime.rs`).** Compiler-side emit produces `file:line:col:` for inline traps, but runtime helpers (`gorget_array_index_oob`, `gorget_str_*_oob`, channel-closed sends, alloc failures, `gorget_panic("integer overflow")` at `c_runtime.rs:5042/5047/5052`, +~45) are called from inside other runtime fns with no caller span → fall back to `<unknown>:0:0:`. Proper fix = per-runtime-function span plumbing (every panic-able entry point grows `(const char* file, int line, int col)`, every emit site passes the LIR instruction's span). Estimate 5-8 days.

- **`caller_location()` builtin + multi-frame stack walking [LOW].** (a) a `#[track_caller]`-like attribute / implicit `caller_location()` so helpers inherit the call site without manual `line, col` plumbing (typed sidecar on `CallExtern`/`Call` carrying caller span, read by the builtin). (b) full backtrace needs DWARF/libunwind in the C backend or LLVM stackmap; async/spawned-task panic locations need the spawning call's span carried through spawn context. Both multi-week; defer until v1 is battle-tested.

- **Typed `Diagnostic` struct in the self-host — sweep remaining stages.** Resolver/parser/typechecker/lexer migrated off `Vector[String] errors` to `Diagnostic`/`DiagKind`; REMAINS:
  - **typechecker — 42 of 47 Rust `self.error(...)` sites unmigrated** (TypeMismatch / WrongArgCount / NoFieldFound / RethrowInNonThrowingFunction / OnErrorInNonThrowingFunction / SelectOutsideAsync / AwaitOutsideAsync / SpawnNonFuture / AwaitNonFuture / NonExhaustiveMatch / PositionalAfterNamed / …). Each needs the check logic ported, not just the diagnostic call.
  - **lowerer:** 1 silent error site (a fixme). Infra inherited via the typechecker migration (symlinks). When lower.gg starts emitting validation diagnostics they can `ctx.diagnostics.push(...)` directly.
  - Follow-up: promote the Diagnostic module to a shared location once cross-directory imports become reasonable (today 4 per-dir copies kept in sync by hand, identical 8-variant DiagKind).

- **Result→T auto-propagation — retire the residual consumer-side `maybe_auto_propagate` safety nets.** Producer-side centralization done (`lower_expr` auto-applies on Call/MethodCall + typechecker gates for if/while/elif/index; Snag #49 holdouts closed). The 7 remaining consumer-side calls (`stmts/assigns.rs:112`, `stmts/mod.rs:129/317/1422`, `calls.rs:151/1030/1065/1206`, `methods.rs:224`, `exprs/mod.rs:1788/1823/1854`) exist for the *Identifier-of-Result* case (`Result[T,E] r = ...; T x = r;`) — the producer hook can't fire on identifiers without breaking `body_r.unwrap()` (the receiver MUST stay `Result`). Principled fix = plumb the typechecker's `expr_types` (post-auto-prop type per span) through monomorphization into IR-lowering, check `op_type` vs `expr_types[span]` at `lower_expr` exit, auto-prop only on disagreement in the throws-sugar direction → retire all 7. Estimate 1-2 days (mostly the monomorphization span-plumbing).

- **Deferred String materialization — Site #4 (borrow-checker decidability) [LOW].** The lifetime question — "can we statically prove `x` doesn't outlive `source`'s last possible mutation?" — needs a separate design pass. Today's `is_cow_unsafe_at(name, span)` heuristic catches the common case but isn't lifetime-aware.

- **Residual: `Option[Box[T]]` / `Result[Box[T]]` field drops not emitted on enum variants and struct fields.** The Box-field-drop wrapper + struct/enum-variant wiring closed 2026-05-01. The Option/Result ENUM-VARIANT skip (`populate_recursive_drop_enums` mod.rs:471-481) + STRUCT-FIELD skip (`populate_recursive_drop_structs` mod.rs:412-422) kept intentional: enabling it crashes the self-host `resolve_stmt` because `stmts.get(i).unwrap()` returns `Stmt` by value (shallow copy aliasing the vector's interior box/string pointers) → double-free. Proper fix is COMPILER-level: make `Vector[T].get(i)` for resource T auto-clone (deep) or return `Ref[T]`-only. Then the skip can be removed + `option_box_enum.gg`'s 3 leaked Some(Box(...)) free correctly. Today: leak (3 blocks), not unsoundness.

- **Self-host silent-fallback audit — IN PROGRESS.** 3 sites emit `/* [bug] ... */` (af0cb513: `map_binop` unknown op, `EIdentifier` unknown name, `EFieldAccess` unknown field) + `map_compound_binop` (299ffb0c). Still to tighten: `infer_method_return_type` I64 fallback for unknown methods, `collection_element_type` "" fallback, `type_id_to_name` "int64_t" fallback for non-GtNamed tids. (A `[bug]`-emission cap was reverted — stage-1's lowerer doesn't reliably propagate mutable global state; loud-by-default, filter via `grep -v '\[bug\]'`.) Proper `GORGET_QUIET_FALLBACKS=1` toggle deferred until the env-var reader lands in the self-host.

- **Stdlib narrow waist — Phase 2c residual.** `Vector.each`/`for_each` void-return entries retired (user-space `lib/std/iter.gg` wrappers). Typed-return Vector HOFs (filter/map/fold/reduce/any/all/find/find_index/count/enumerate/flat_map/zip) STAY as BuiltinMethodDecl entries — IR-lowering reads their declared return types via `resolve_builtin_method_return_type` when the user-space wrapper sig isn't registered yet (early generic mono). Full retirement blocks on a separate signature source for IR-lowering when BuiltinMethodDecl is absent. Dict.each/Set.each BuiltinMethodDecls stay (no wrapper migration). LIR `HofOp` variants stay (serve Dict/Set too).

- **Self-host check_comparison residual gaps — 8 mismatches** [1013/1021 = 99.2%]: (a) Type-variable preservation (~5: `coroutine_collections`/`generic_pair_swap`/`httpserver_middleware`/`httpserver_router_extended`/`test_vector_bool`/`test_vector_edge_cases` — Rust keeps numbered inference vars at unresolved closure-param call sites; self-host concretises or `<error>`); (b) Function-type parser String-aliasing (1: `generic_callable.gg`'s `Callable[<error>(int)]`); (c) misc one-offs (closure_tuple_destructure, sigil_type_args [the `int !`/`String &` iterator-intent sigil rendering + `?;` per assoc-type], copy_struct_closure_capture). Most gains are architecturally deep; score is stable, further closure may be net-negative ROI vs the layering migrations in `docs/devbook/26`.

- **C backend: retire local val_types/ptr_pointee fixup phases (after the 2026-05-15 seed migration).** Seeds `val_types`/`ptr_pointee` from `func.value_types`/`func.pointee_types`; remaining cross-instruction reasoning to push upstream so the local pass disappears: Guard accessor inference from consumers (`mod.rs:1381-1456`), cross-type Option/Result map combinator override (`mod.rs:1465-1486` via `combinator_result_struct_id`), consumer-driven Add/Cmp peer-type back-propagation (`mod.rs:1490-1570`), InlineC→SlotStore type inference (`mod.rs:1306-1322`), Ret-from-function backfill. Each needs `compute_module_value_types` to gain a fixed-point/multi-phase walk. Half-day each + tests.

- **Decompose emit_call_extern.rs (~908 lines).** Tier 1-3 + HOF (→`emit_hof.rs`) + printf (→`emit_printf.rs`) lifted. Remaining genuinely-backend-specific clusters: out-parameter adaptation (~178 lines), collection drop/clone injection (~70 lines).

- **Phase A's `resources` build-tooling — the cross-language single source of truth** (`docs/devbook/18-runtime-abi.md` + `26-self-host-frontend.md`). Canonical source = idiomatic Gorget code at `compiler/data/{schema,resources}.gg`, baked via `include_str!` + parsed by the compiler's own parser (no build.rs/codegen; self-host imports the same files). Items 1-6 done (runtime_extern_sig retirement, CollectionKind close, spike, Rust loader + AST walker, self-host adoption, self-host `starts_with` consumer-read retirement). Remaining:
  - **Item 7 — retire Rust name-prefix dispatch sites.** PARTIAL (first wave: `ResourceKind::RefCounted` + 3 sites migrated to `resources::table().lookup(name)` reading typed `collection_kind`). Site survey: `starts_with("[A-Z][a-zA-Z]*__"` = 256 hits (TODO's old "9" was the headline). Sub-items (7e is load-bearing — 7b + most 7d retire once it lands):
    - **7b. `emit_collection_constructor`** (`helpers.rs:824-915`) — typed Tier 1 landed (7e Phase 4 `4aa0cdcb`); Tier 2 name-parse fallback retires once 7e-r1 makes Resource operands universal.
    - **7d. residual `starts_with` sites** (`emit_types.rs`, `validate.rs`, `lifts.rs`, `mod.rs`) — mostly WRITER stages reading element/key/value types from mangled names before val_types is computed. Bundled under 7e-r1 + 7e-r3.
    - **7e. LIR element-type parametricity — the writer-site fix that retires name-parsing as a CLASS.** Encoding done (`LirType::Resource { kind, params }` SSoT + `expected_resource_arity`/`validate_resource_arity`; `emit_collection_constructor` prefers typed reads). Residue (all gated on extending the writer-side `Resource` propagation beyond `Inst::CollectionCtor` results): **7e-r1 global writer flip** (`map_gir_type_with_structs` returns `Resource{kind,params}` for every resource-type GIR Named operand; ~64 c_lir `LirType::Struct(sid)` sites need parallel `Resource` arms; helpers `parametric_resource_lir_type`+`classify_resource_name` recoverable from `2a337b6a..490a45b2`); **7e-r3 Phase 5 fallback retirement** (once r1 universal, delete `emit_collection_constructor` Tier 2 + `elem_size_from_monomorphized`/`dict_elem_sizes_from_monomorphized`/`concurrency_elem_size` name-reads in `types.rs`). Encoding (a) `Resource{kind, params:SmallVec<[LirType;2]>}` is the conceptually-clean path (perf to be benchmarked, not assumed); (b) per-mono StructId is the alternative. Spike (a) for half a day + measure compile-time on the lowerer driver before deciding. Estimate 3-5 days, design-attention, schedule deliberately.

Affects `self` AND identifier roots IDENTICALLY (the self-root fix made self consistent with this pre-existing local behavior, not worse). Should the for-loop check adopt the same field-path disjointness (reuse `find_root_def_id_with_path` + `paths_overlap`) so `for x in c.a: c.b.push()` is accepted? - **🆕 [D10(b) self-root follow-up, filed 2026-07-14 — OWNER QUESTION] Moving a field out of a bare (borrowed) `self` (`take(!self.items)`) stays accepted.** A partial-move-out-of-borrowed-self case — related to the D12/partial-move family and the undecided destructuring-partial-move widening (2026-07-11 D10(a) ADDENDUM). Separate from the self-root place-overlap fix; needs an owner ruling on whether/how partial self-moves are checked. Own scout when the partial-move family is worked.
- **🆕 [D10(b) B1 follow-up, filed 2026-07-14] Disjoint-sibling MOVE `f(!m.a, !m.b)` over-rejects — undecided partial-move widening.** Moving two DISJOINT sibling sub-places today trips `E_UseAfterMove` because the move-tracker root-marks the whole DefId `m` on the first `!m.a`. Whether this SHOULD accept (Rust-style destructuring partial move) is the WIDENING left UNDECIDED by the 2026-07-11 D10(a) ADDENDUM (`decisions.md`). D10(b) place-overlap deliberately keeps `(Move,Move)` out (E_DoubleMove/E_UseAfterMove own it), so this is NOT B1's call. Fixture documents the question: `tests/fixtures/place_overlap_disjoint_sibling_move_error.gg` + the `#[ignore]`d test `place_overlap_disjoint_sibling_move_error`. Needs an OWNER RULING; if widening is ruled in, flip the fixture to a build+run POSITIVE and relax the projection-move root-marking to path-precise.
- **🆕 [D23/T3a follow-up, filed 2026-07-10, MEDIUM-design] General must-use on `Result`.** D23/T3a pins ONLY that an unhandled `throws` call in a CONSUME position rejects (`E_UnhandledThrows`). It does NOT catch a `Result` VALUE that is captured then ignored — `auto r = throws_call()` bound but never matched, or a `Result`-returning fn's value discarded even inside a `throws` fn. Whether Gorget wants a general `#[must_use]`-style must-use-on-`Result` (warn or error on a dropped `Result`) is a separate language-design question — deliberately OUT of D23 scope (owner Q4 ruling). Design + owner-decide before implementing; pairs with the error-model residuals.

- **🧹 [round-33 alloc scout filing — DIRECTION RATIFIED 2026-07-06 as D13 TWO-STEP (decisions.md LOG): step 1 = REJECT bare allocator locals at check + docs rewrite, briefable now (resources-scout evidence /tmp/recover_scout_resources/); step 2 = full RAII drop-registration w/ borrow_deps ordering, filed target. The 'Decide:' below is DECIDED.] Bare (non-`with`) allocator locals are never destroyed — guaranteed leak class.** `Arena a = Arena(4096)` outside `with` is lowered via plain `call_extern` (no drop registration, `calls.rs` allocator-ctor branches) and there is no scope-exit destroy → ASan LeakSanitizer reports 80B handle + 4096B block (regenerate: bare-arena probe with either Vector or String `alloc=`; identical shape both). PRE-EXISTING for collections (the docs' own §15.3 `alloc=` example uses a bare PoolAllocator). Also NOTE: at arena_depth 0 the escape gate doesn't track bare-allocator-backed values (`check_stmt.rs:194` gates on `arena_depth > 0`), so today the leak is what makes the bare idiom "safe". Decide: drop-register allocator handles (destroy at scope exit + extend escape tracking to bare allocators) or document the leak as the bare-idiom contract. **ESCALATION (round-33 allocator-class scout, 2026-07-02, ASan-verified): not just a leak — manual `.destroy()` of a bare allocator BEFORE scope exit while an RAII value backed by it is still live is a heap-UAF from safe code.** `Arena a = Arena(4096)` + `String s = String("…long…", alloc=a)` + `a.destroy()` → the scope-exit drop of `s` frees through the destroyed arena → ASan heap-use-after-free in `gorget_string_free` (the plain run prints fine — silent). The decide-branch must also cover destroy ordering: drop-registration with ordering fixes both; merely documenting the leak contract does NOT fix this one. (The round's `allocator_ctor_alloc_keyword.gg` fixture uses the with-idiom specifically to sidestep this.)

- **🐛 SELF-HOST MISCOMPILES in-place `dict.get(k).unwrap().push(v)` → SIGSEGV stage-1 binary, CONTEXT-DEPENDENT** (discovered 2026-06-28 by the `implements`-predicate scout). Mutating a `Vector` obtained from a `Dict.get(k).unwrap()` IN PLACE and relying on the dict seeing the mutation (the in-place idiom `gmod.trait_impls_by_type.get(k).unwrap().push(v)`) mis-compiles into a faulting stage-1 binary specifically in the `lower_module` pre-pass context (`tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lower.gg`, the trait-impl-registry build loop). The SAME idiom compiles fine elsewhere in the self-host (`lir_ssa`/`scope`/`lir_codegen`), so it is context-dependent — likely an alias/temp-lifetime interaction between the `Dict.get().unwrap()` view and an in-place `.push()` reallocation in this particular function/scope shape. The `implements` pre-pass DODGES it via rebuild-then-put (`get → owned local → push → put(k, !local)`); the miscompile itself is UNFIXED. Repro: revert the rebuild-then-put in `lower_module`'s `trait_impls_by_type` loop to the in-place idiom and run `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` → stage-1 SIGSEGV. Proper fix lives in the self-host lowerer's view-vs-owned / `.get().unwrap()` aliasing path (mirror Rust's treatment); until then keep the rebuild-then-put shape at this site.

Hand-synced parallel lists (typing-coercion vs emit) that desynced on `path_absolute` → a bootstrap break (fixed `fef40817` by adding the arm; root not removed). CLAUDE.md "one source of truth per axis".

- **🐛 SELF-HOST MISLOWERS `None()` (with parens) → mistyped `Option[int]` temp** (#14 P0). The PARSER accepts both `None()` and bare `None` (`self_host_parser/parser.gg:1478`), but the LOWERER lowers a bare-variant CALL `None()` as a constructor defaulting to `Option[int]` regardless of context → mistyped dead temp → stage-1 cc fails (`incompatible types … int64_t from Option__int64_t`). Bare `None` lowers correctly (type flows from context). Rust gg tolerates `None()` → `fixed_point`-only. PROPER FIX = infer `None()`'s Option type from the consuming position (route through the same context-typed None path); OR (lesser) reject `None()` at check time. Likely the same root as `project_selfhost_none_typing_bug`.

- **📖 `docs/book/12-borrowing.md` §"MutationWhileBorrowed" is REFUTED-BY-RUN.** The section claims `auto entry = v.get(0).unwrap()` + `v.push(42)` is a compile ERROR — it compiles + runs today (the for-loop sub-case still errors as documented). UNRESOLVED design question before rewriting: the in-place mutable-borrow shape (`auto p = players.get(0).unwrap(); p.score += 10; players.push(...); p.score += 100`) compiles AND keeps writing through after the push (run-proven 11/111/111) — if the push reallocs, that write-through is a latent UAF. Decide intent first (CoW value binds = allowed carve-out vs in-place `&`-element borrows = still-rejected), THEN fix EITHER the check or the chapter — don't bless current behavior until the safety story is confirmed.

- **🐛 LLVM backend: `--sanitize` is SILENTLY DROPPED — an "LLVM ASan" run is VACUOUS.** `compile_llvm_pipeline` (`src/main.rs:1177`) never reads `options.sanitize`; the LLVM clang/link path passes nothing (binary has zero asan symbols). Any LLVM-side memory gate must use STDOUT PARITY with the C backend. Fix: at minimum warn loudly when `--sanitize` + `--backend=llvm`; properly pass `-fsanitize=address,undefined` through the LLVM link step.

- **🐛 LLVM backend: in-loop sret/temp allocas emitted PER ITERATION → a print-heavy loop stack-exhausts (SIGSEGV) at ~399k bool-prints** (pre-existing). Each iteration's `alloca %GorgetString` is at its use site inside the loop → frame grows with trip count (C backend has block-scoped autos, no cliff). Fix = hoist allocas to the function entry block (also defeats mem2reg otherwise). Until then keep LLVM loop probes under ~100k prints.


- **(rule-2 debt) `infer_collection_element_type` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs`) decides element types from NAME PREFIXES** (`Vector__`/`Dict__`/`Map__`). Element-type metadata should be a typed field on the collection TypeDef (`element_type` axis set at `register_collection_alias`/`ensure_collection_type`), read via an accessor. The String-base comprehension no longer reaches it; every other caller still keys on the prefix (a new collection family falls to I64). Fix the axis, not the next caller.

- **#13 borrow-default STEP 2 (field-path RSS) — cheap path REFUTED (leaf-borrow probe KILL). NEXT = array_clone ATTRIBUTION scout → then the alias-sever DECISION (owner-worthy: a major chain).** The hot field-path `.get().unwrap()` reads bind VALUE aggregates (`GirType`/`GirLocal`), NOT GtPtr-resource deref-clones → Branch C-pre never fires there → borrowing them needs the full alias-sever (make the VALUE-aggregate bind a reference, not a copy). Before that double-free-prone cornerstone, a deeper scout MUST ATTRIBUTE the ~19.4M array_clone to call-sites (instrument the clone path on a STAGE-1 run): value-aggregate `.get()` binds (alias-sever target) vs get-mutate-set Vector clones (`ctx.locals.set`) vs explicit `.clone()`. (`array_new=22.1M`/`string_cat=5.1M`/`live_bytes≈5.6GB` suggest collection-growth + value-copy.) The cheap borrow-default wins are EXHAUSTED; the ③(d)-root RSS win (≈4.9GB → near Rust) is the alias-sever, weigh vs leak-freedom + parity. Harness `/tmp/measure_stage1_probe.sh`.

NOT the print-temp class — each an other-class drop-registration gap. RUN an ASan witness per fixture, trace the unregistered materialization, register at the producer.

Register the call result at the producer in trait-dispatch lowering. (Box-related residue may be a second gap.)

- **🐛 Print-temp site-C ungated sub-shape: a Clone-of-NON-string-AGGREGATE f-string interp temp stays UNREGISTERED** (deliberate gate in `lower_interp_segment` branches 2/3, `calls.rs`). Registered only under Move or Clone-of-String, because `AssignMode::Clone` on a non-string aggregate is a SHALLOW memcpy today (registering the alias would double-free). If such an aggregate owns heap, it leaks. Unblocks when Clone deep-copies aggregates — then drop the `is_string_type` half at both branches.

- **🐛 LATENT DCE gap: `has_side_effects` (`src/lir/optimize.rs:576`) omits `Inst::CallRuntime`** (lists `CallExtern`/`Call`/`CallPtr`/`CallClosure` but NOT `CallRuntime`). A value-returning `CallRuntime` with an UNUSED dst AND real side effects would be wrongly DCE-eliminated. VOID `CallRuntime`s are SAFE (`inst.dst()` is None → kept). Fix = add `Inst::CallRuntime { .. }` to the `matches!` set. Verify no perf regression.

- **🐛 LATENT: `is_collection_assignment` accepts ANY array/set literal regardless of ELEMENT type.** `is_collection_assignment` (`src/semantic/typecheck.rs:3800`) returns true for any `ResolvedType::Array(_,_)` against a `Vector`/`Dict`/`Set`/`HashMap`/`HashSet` dest WITHOUT checking the element type → `Vector[int] v = ["a", "b"]` + `Vector[int] f(): return ["a", "b"]` both pass `gg check` then misbehave. Pre-existing in VarDecl; the Return/Assign extension inherits the laxity (doesn't introduce it). Fix = unify (or check-compatible) the literal's element type against the dest's declared element type. One helper; affects VarDecl + Return + Assign.

- **`Ordinal` built-in trait never registered → wrong-signature `equip X with Ordinal:` compiles clean** (latent validation gap). `builtin_traits` carries `("Ordinal", …)` with `int ordinal(self)` (`src/semantic/traits.rs:603`), but registration is gated on `scopes.lookup(name)` (`traits.rs:824`) and `Ordinal` is absent from the resolver's reservation list (`src/semantic/resolve.rs:127`) + no `lib/` declares `trait Ordinal` → the entry is dropped, the registry never holds an Ordinal `TraitInfo` → `equip X with Ordinal:` falls to the inherent-impl path (`trait_def_id=None`), signature NEVER validated (`@derive(Ordinal)` desugars to one, `derive.rs:87`). Repro: wrong-sig `equip Color with Ordinal:` → "OK"; same on `Comparable` is correctly rejected. Fix: add `"Ordinal"` to `resolve.rs:127` (cheapest), OR declare `trait Ordinal` in a lib. Both book (`appendix-traits.md:92`) + spec (`language-reference.md:~2705`/`~2783`) document it as real.

- **Compiler footguns (latent type-system validation gaps, same class as `Ordinal`):**
  - **(C3) Phantom static methods pass `gg check`, fail at C build/link.** `String.from("x")`/`int.from(5)`/`int.try_parse(s)` all report "OK" but `gg build` fails (implicit-decl / undefined-reference / phantom symbol). Root: `resolve_static_method_type` (`typecheck.rs:4717`, called `:1740`) returns `None` for an unknown `Type.method()` + the call FALLS THROUGH → the backend name-mangles into a nonexistent runtime symbol (rule-2 violation). Fix: emit a `NoSuchMethod`/`UnknownStaticMethod` diagnostic at typecheck. Canonical working: `String("x")`, `int.parse(s)`→Option, `parse_int(s)`→Result.
  - **`.unwrap()`/`.expect()`/`.unwrap_or()` on a NON-Option/Result is a silent no-op pass-through** (`methods.rs:588`, the `matches!(method_name, "unwrap"|"expect"|"unwrap_or")` arm returns the receiver unchanged). A genuine type confusion (e.g. `channel_exec(...).unwrap()` on a plain `CommandResult`) is silently swallowed. Fix (design call; verify no stdlib/self-host relies on the no-op first): make it a typecheck error or at minimum a lint.

- **Remove name-matching from consuming-position lowering — add an EXPLICIT per-method consuming-param signal (hardened by reviewer passes 1+2).** `lower_method_call` (`src/ir/lowering/exprs/methods.rs:1850-1861`, `consuming_positions_by_name`) identifies consuming arg positions by method NAME (push/add/extend/send/push_back/push_front→0; put/set/insert→0,1) + `is_string_builder_method` exclusion. Feeds FOUR name-matched consumers: `pre_call_clone_temps` (~:1908), `move_zero_locals` (~:2143), `consuming_clone_temps` (~:2235), `value_arg_idx_for_method` (:1822-1825). A 2026-05-25 band-aid (`07649296`) added a `ParamABI::ByPtr` filter (closed the leak, left the name match). **Current state is CORRECT** — this is a cleanliness refactor (+ closes pre-existing `get_or_put`/`fill`/`extend` gaps), NOT a bug fix.
  - **PROPER fix: add `consumes_params: &'static [usize]` to `BuiltinMethodDecl`** (arg positions whose ownership transfers), set per-method ONLY for true transfers: array push[1]/insert[2]/set[2]/extend[1]/fill[2], map put key+value[1,2], set add[1]/insert[1], heap push[1], channel send[1], guard/write_guard set[1]. EMPTY for `update` (clones), `sort*` (closure), `contains`/`get`/`remove` (read-only).
  - **PASS-1's `is_mutating` trigger is UNSOUND — confirmed false-positive LEAKS** (`Dict.update` `is_mutating:true` but `gorget_map_update`→`gorget_map_put_cloned` CLONES; `Vector.sort*` `is_mutating:true` but no consumed element). `is_mutating` ≠ ownership-transfer. The by-void-ptr table `collection_void_param_indices` (`helpers.rs:681`) is a SUPERSET incl. read-only `contains`/`get`/`remove` — cross-check for position ACCURACY only, don't copy wholesale.
  - **INDEXING (off-by-one):** `consumes_params` is self-INCLUSIVE 1-based (matches `move_indices`/`fn_param_ownerships[i+1]`); the sibling `params` field is self-EXCLUSIVE. State in the field doc.
  - **CHURN:** `BuiltinMethodDecl` has 180 `&'static` literals + `params`/`return_type` are fn-pointers (`#[derive(Default)]` IMPOSSIBLE) → land a declarative builder-macro / hand-`impl Default` FIRST, then set `consumes_params` on ~11 entries.
  - **Then:** (1) drive `register_collection_method_sigs` ownership off this field; (2) derive `consuming_positions` from `ownership==Move`, delete all 4 name-matched sites + `is_string_builder_method` + the `ByPtr` filter. For Dict source key/value `TypeId`s from `GenericCollector::instances_raw()` (`generics/mod.rs:2225`), NOT mangled-name splitting.
  - **Non-collection builtins (Channel/Guard/WriteGuard) need their OWN registration path** (verified they never reach `register_collection_method_sigs` — `scan_type` `generics/mod.rs:~390` registers only Vector/Dict/HashMap/Set/HashSet/Box). EITHER add these bases to the `scan_type` allowlist OR give the new path its own mangled-instance source. Read `consumes_params` via `lookup_protocol(base)`/`protocol_for_mangled_name` (`builtins.rs:~989`). Register `fn_param_ownerships`(+`fn_param_abis`) ONLY — NOT `fn_sigs` (return-type-resolution precedence at `methods.rs:~2051`); `populate_consume_externs` (`mod.rs:1860`) picks up the new Move entries automatically.
  - **⚠ Blast radius:** `consume_externs` feeds the FATAL Tier-2a validator (`mod.rs:~1870`) — a false-positive `Move` becomes a COMPILE error (fails loud, but test for it).
  - **Gates (the obvious ones are BLIND; existing leak fixtures use only PRIMITIVE int):** add resource-`T` leak-probe fixtures for Dict put/set, **`Channel[String].send`** (highest-risk), Guard/WriteGuard `[String].set`, Vector extend/fill, Set insert; AND a `Dict.update(other_with_resource_values)` probe asserting NO leak; AND a `Dict.get_or_put` MISS-path probe with a resource-typed default (the default is both `gorget_map_put`'d AND SlotStored on miss — characterize, don't fix here). Deque: protocol-table only (not constructable). Revert if it regresses.
