- **`--clones=stats` per-CloneId runtime breakdown.** Compile-time half landed (`CloneId` join-key; `--clones=verbose` renders per-site). Runtime side: thread `CloneId` from `ImplicitCloneWarning` through LIR lowering to each runtime clone-call emit, allocate a per-id `_Atomic size_t` counter array, bump at each call, atexit emits a per-id breakdown. The hardcoded `call_extern("gorget_panic", …)` at `src/ir/lowering/stmts/mod.rs:2132` for `assert` remains. Option (a): declare `panic` in a stdlib module as `extern noreturn void panic(String msg)`, route `assert` through a normal call, retire the name-match. Defer until the prelude/auto-import machinery can make `panic` globally available without an explicit import.

## Low

- **🆕 [LOW — self-host over-reject, DORMANT; from the flip-tracks landing 2026-07-17, cross-refs the RV-B DotShorthand entry] The single-owner-init reject MISFIRES on a legal `.Wrap(!callable)` enum-shorthand.** The EDotShorthand parser adapter (`self_host_typechecker/parser.gg:~2553`) pushes only `dsa.value` and DISCARDS the `!` move sigil, so `reject_single_owner_init` sees a bare EIdentifier and would fire a self-contradicting "write `!callable` to move" on code that ALREADY wrote `!`. Dormant (SELFHOST 214/214 + bootstrap green ⇒ no corpus hit) and over-reject-only. Clears when RV-B's sigil-preserving adapter lands. Note is at the EDotShorthand safety-walk arm in `typecheck.gg`.
- **🆕 [LOW — self-host over-reject, DORMANT + UNREACHABLE; from the flip-tracks landing 2026-07-17] The self-host EStructLiteral single-owner-init arm lacks production's ref-typed-field skip** (`check_expr.rs:1183` `!target_is_ref` skips a `Ref[Callable]` field init — a borrow, not a copy). This arm rejects ALL args with no ref-field gate → would OVER-REJECT the exotic `S{cbref: f}` (cbref borrow-typed). Over-reject-only (never UAF); the arm is also structurally UNREACHABLE today (the parser never builds EStructLiteral — `S(...)` is ECall, `{...}` a dict/set). The per-field ref-flag mirror (`compute_struct_field_ref_flags`) is deferred. Note is at the EStructLiteral safety-walk arm in `typecheck.gg`.
- **🆕🐛 [HIGH — SILENT WRONG OUTPUT today, from the 1C brief-review pass-1 2026-07-16] Rust `HashMap`-of-struct ELEMENT TYPING is broken: `infer_collection_element_type` (`methods.rs:3859`) strips `Dict__`/`Map__` but NOT `HashMap__` → `HashMap__int64_t__Point` resolves to I64 (fallback `:3871`).** Measured: `HashMap[int,Point]` field READ `d[0].x` prints `0` (pre-existing, no 1C involvement); whole-element read `Point p = d[0]` C-COMPILE-FAILS; scalar `HashMap[int,int]` works. ⚠ ASYMMETRY: the self-host twin `index_value_type_name` (`lower_types.gg:2648`) DOES handle `HashMap__` — Rust is less complete than its own mirror. The 1C write-gate was SCOPED to `Array|OrderedMap` because of this (the Map arm would silently route HashMap field-writes into wrong output); **this track's fix also flips the 1C gate arm to include `Map` + adds the HashMap shape to the 1C fixture** (one-line flip once the typing is fixed — verify construction/put end-to-end first, the cc-fail suggests the mis-typing is wider than reads). **⚠ ASYMMETRY NOTE (1C pass-3 2026-07-16): post-1C the SELF-HOST already writes through HashMap-of-struct fields** (it has no CkHashMap — `resource_meta_for` maps `HashMap__`→`CkDict`, `lir_lower.gg:318`, and `index_value_type_name` resolves HashMap values, `lower_types.gg:2648`) **while scoped Rust prints 0 — the HashMap read/store is ITSELF broken by the element-typing bug (methods.rs:3859), independent of the write-through gate (a store-then-read with NO field write also prints 0; 1C pass-5 measured) — a LATENT Rust≠self-host divergence (zero corpus sites today). This track closes it by fixing RUST (strip `HashMap__` at methods.rs:3859 + admit `Map` in the 1C gate), restoring parity.** Own scout→brief→gauntlet.
- **🆕🧹 [MED-LOW — typed-metadata smell in OUR OWN test infra, self-filed 2026-07-16] The ggdef corpus out-of-subset mechanism is TWO name-keyed EXCLUDE lists (`corpus_b.rs` + `corpus_b1.rs`) — sibling drift by construction** (the 1B fixture needed both edited; 1C will again; a missed twin = a bricked lane, exactly the parallel-list class rule 2 forbids). - **🐛 (G1 follow-up, PRE-EXISTING) N1 — `while: v.push(x)` on a non-materialized value-param LOSES the pushes.** Reproduces at base `d68afcf5`; memory-SAFE; CoW loop-materialize-scoping limitation (lazy re-materialize per iter); wrong-result not UAF; unrolled pushes fine, only the loop form; the shape WITH a preceding projected store now correctly accumulates on the private copy post-G1.
- **🐛 (G1 follow-up, PRE-EXISTING) N2 — closure-return-String 2-byte/call leak.** `Res r=Res("A"); auto cb=(): r.name; print(cb())` leaks 2 bytes/call (C LSan), no vector/CoW; reproduces at base.
- **🧹 (G1 follow-up) lint-file-scope: widen `g1_projected_materialize_sites_untrack` files[] IF a projected-materialize cow_before_mutation site is ever added to calls.rs/mod.rs.** Today adequate — the 4 callers outside methods.rs/assigns.rs (calls.rs:59/97/336, exprs/mod.rs:260) are whole-value materializes (safe-by-construction).
- **🐛 Local mutable-borrow binding is BROKEN + `&`-exclusivity UNENFORCED (owner-requested verification 2026-07-02; NOT urgent — reject eventually).** The spec makes `&` an EXCLUSIVE mutable borrow (`language-design.md:574-575` / `language-reference.md:2276-2277`: "any number of immutable borrows OR exactly one mutable borrow"; `reference §7.15` `:1747`: "the original ... cannot be accessed while the borrow is active"). The impl does not enforce it, and local `&`-bindings don't even produce a live borrow. Verified on `2194bd27` (`gg check`/`run`): `auto a = &b; b[1]=99; print(a[1])` → check OK, **runs to GARBAGE** (`auto a=&b` MISCOMPILES — Core #8 accept-and-miscompile); `Vector[int] &a = b; b[1]=99; print(a[1])` → check OK, prints the ORIGINAL `2` (`a` is a COPY, not a live alias). INTENDED (owner + spec): **REJECT** the code — `b` is inaccessible while mutably borrowed by `a` (borrow-exclusivity error); binding a mutable borrow to a local should either be that live-exclusive-borrow or be rejected as an unsupported form. Orthogonal to the full-lazy-CoW materialize track (this is borrow-check/lowering). When tackled: wire a `#[ignore]`d negative fixture (expected: reject) encoding the intent, then implement. Repros captured in session scratch.

- **(durable end-state for the cstr-return registry — STOPGAP shipped `2026-06-14`) Retire the `runtime_fn_returns_cstr` name-list in favor of TYPED cstr provenance.** The axis is single-sourced in `runtime_fn_returns_cstr` (`tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lir.gg`), both wrap mechanisms delegating, kept honest by `tests/lints.rs cstr_return_registry_single_source` — but still a NAME LIST. Durable fix = carry cstr-ness as TYPED return-ABI provenance on the call/runtime-decl (mirror Rust `ValueOrigin::CStr`/`AbiKind::CStr`, `src/lir/types.rs:982`), retiring the name-list + both predicates + the lint.

- **🔵 TAIL-CALL OPTIMIZATION (TCO) — LATER (owner-set; a real language feature).** gg has no TCO. **Phase 1 (tractable, high-value): self-tail-recursion → loop** — detect a tail-position call to the SAME function (`return f(args)` returned DIRECTLY; ⚠ `return deep(n-1)+1` is NOT tail) THROUGH if/match/block tails, lower to param-rebind + jump-to-entry. Both compilers (Rust GIR/LIR + self-host `lower.gg`). **Phase 2 (defer): general/sibling tail calls** (`musttail`/trampoline). **Design Qs FIRST:** (a) GUARANTEED (Scheme-like, stack-safe — recommended given the safety pillar) vs best-effort; (b) ⚠ DROP/CoW ordering hazard — the tail jump REUSES the frame, so dead droppable locals must drop BEFORE the jump + a live droppable that outlives the call BLOCKS the TCO (UAF/leak otherwise); (c) the `assert return` postcondition runs AT the return. Non-tail deep recursion (`stack_guard_deep_recursion`) intentionally NOT covered (owner chose honest OS-default-stack). Gate: a tail-recursive fixture runs at 1e7+ depth on an 8MB stack; `fixed_point` GREEN; no ASan drop/leak regression.

- **🔵 BENCHMARK COVERAGE GAP: the bench corpus does NOT exercise #37 lazy CoW.** A clean A/B (eager vs `GG_COW_LAZY_LOOP=1` at `09030b7f`) over `tools/bench_snapshot.sh`'s corpus showed full lazy materialization is a byte-identical no-op (C/allocs/RSS/wall-time neutral) — NONE of the bench fixtures contain the pattern lazy targets (a `String` bound to a collection-element VIEW whose collection is mutated on a forward path in the same scope/loop). Confirmed only in purpose-built witnesses (below bench/RSS granularity, a 5-byte string). To SIZE the #37 win, ADD a bench fixture in the loop-carried-string-mutation (witness) shape sized to millions of iterations. Also `array clone=0` across the corpus → no fixture exercises ARRAY CoW (Phase 2/collection CoW will want coverage).

- **(Deferred) Reimplement `EntityPool` atop a value-less `SlotMap`.** Optional follow-up to the ECS-onto-SlotKey unification. `EntityPool` (free-list + generations in `lib/xtd/ecs.gg`) duplicates `SlotMap`'s allocation; a value-less `SlotMap` could share it. No blocker — `Entity` is already `type Entity = SlotKey`; `SparseSet[T]` stays a deliberately-dense component store (do NOT fold).

- **🔧 (self-host) Phase 2c COMMIT 3 — drop-emission flip (keystone).** Prereqs done (drop_elab packed-2-bit `1289a7d7`, GIMoveZero step-1, Site 2a, Fix-D struct-ctor `fn_move_params` `69c80d64`, Prereq B/C, Phase-3 `add_local_inheriting` `9f955c5c`). - **SELF-HOST lexer bug (NOT a Rust bug): string literal inside an f-string interpolation truncates the enclosing `match`.** A nested string literal in an f-string interpolation (`f"... {f(\"add\", x)} ..."`) is mis-lexed: the `\"` inside `{...}` ends the f-string early → everything after that `case` arm is silently dropped (nasty: no parse error at the site, a downstream non-exhaustive-match error one function over + a phantom extern). The Rust compiler does NOT have this (`src/lexer/mod.rs:691-694` calls `skip_quoted_string` which skips `\\` escapes). Fix the self-host interpolation-segment scanner (`tests/fixtures/self_host_*/lex.gg`) to mirror `skip_quoted_string`. Workaround: bind the inner call to a `String` local first.

## [DEEP track — NOT single-zone] Full loop-else (for-else all paths + while-else)
Loop-else scout (round-8) proved the full feature is NOT bounded to lower_loops.gg. Ship the zone-clean `lower_for_iterator` slice first (+1 `iterable`); the REST is a dedicated serialized broad track:
- **for-else** across `lower_for_range`(:701)/`lower_for_vector`(:224)/`lower_for_dict`(:491)/`lower_for_string`(:325) — mechanism proven (copy `lower_for_set` lower_loops.gg:606-688: `else_bb=exit_bb; if else_body.len()>0: else_bb=new_block`, header-false/`next()→None` edge → else_bb, break pushes exit_bb so it skips else, emit else tail → jump exit). Centralize via a shared `emit_loop_else_tail(...)` helper. DELETE the dispatcher `lower_fail` guards at lower_loops.gg:176-177 (vector) + :188-189 (dict) as each path gains real handling. SPILLS into `lower_expr.gg` (12 comprehension call-sites: 3×range, 7×vector, 1×dict, 1×string need an empty-`[]` else arg).
- **while-else** = DEEP cross-file AST-arity change: the gap is at the PARSER (`parser.gg:2962-2965` parses then DISCARDS the while-else block), and `SWhile(SpannedExpr, Vector[Stmt])` (`ast.gg:149`) has NO else field. Needs: ast.gg new field + parser bind/emit + ~14 `case SWhile(...)` match sites (meta.gg×4, lower_generics.gg×8, lower_closures.gg×4, lower_cow.gg, resolve.gg×2, typecheck.gg, format.gg, loader.gg×4, lower.gg×2). This is the load-bearing blocker for `for_else` + `break_nested`.
- Rust ref: `src/ir/lowering/stmts/for_loops.rs` `alloc_for_blocks`(:33-44)+`emit_else_arm_tail`(:68-81); `mod.rs:2290-2343` (while dual-target). Docs: `docs/language-reference.md:1276` (else runs on natural exhaustion, break skips it).
- Yield when COMPLETE: `iterable`(+via slice) + `for_else` + `break_nested` = +3 total. Bootstrap-safe (self-host source has zero loop-else).

`resolve_field_gir_type` (lower_types.gg) keeps `byte` on the signed arm (`int8_t|i8|byte→I8_TYPE`), but Rust treats `byte` as a `uint8` alias (`src/lexer/token.rs:471`, `src/ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs:2566`) and the sibling `prim_name_to_type` (lower_types.gg:56-58) correctly maps `byte→U8_TYPE`. Verify no fixture flips before/after (should be a no-op).

## [follow-up, from none_literal review] map_ast_type registration side-effect is a latent footgun
`map_ast_type` calls `lookup_or_register_named` (lower_types.gg:~645) — a REGISTRATION SIDE EFFECT, so it is NOT a pure type-query. The fn_param_sigs pre-pass guard `not fsdef.is_extern_stub` (lower.gg:3550) LOCALLY contains it for stub PARAMS, but the unguarded return-path registration (`map_fn_return_type`→`map_ast_type`, lower.gg:3555) would re-trip it for a future `extern Guard[bool] make()` (a stub whose RETURN is a named resource). Principled fix: split a side-effect-free pure type-query path from the register-on-demand path, OR make the pre-pass use the query-only variant. Not urgent (no current fixture trips the return path), but the footgun is real.

## [CLASS FIX — layering, from Increment-2 scout + Round-8 Track A] typed is_borrow_ptr flag on GirFieldInfo
Multiple sites reconstruct "is this field a borrow pointer" from the field-type-NAME prefix `Ref__`/`MutRef__` (core-invariant-#2 smell): `lir_lower.gg:4360` GIFieldLoad dispatch (Round-8 Track A added the `MutRef__` read), `resolve_field_lir_type:708`, `resolve_field_gir_type:942`, and now closure Increment-2's ByMutRef env field (which must be NAMED `MutRef__<T>` to take the load-stored-pointer path). All reuse the documented accepted residual because `GirFieldInfo` stores only a type-name String, not a typed id. CLEAN CLASS FIX: add a typed `bool is_borrow_ptr` (or a typed pointee-id) to `GirFieldInfo` (gir.gg), set it at field registration, and read it at all the above sites instead of the name prefix — retiring the whole residual class. Mirrors Rust's typed `field_is_ptr` (insts.rs:1029). Medium track (gir.gg + ~4 consumers + careful review); do AFTER the closure increments that depend on the current residual behavior have landed (so the cleanup is a pure no-op refactor). This subsumes the earlier "MutRef__ symmetry" nit.

## [defensive, from Increment-2 review] is_move guard on ByMutRef reclassify
The ByMutRef reclassify (lower_expr.gg:~3186) emits a `MutPtr(&outer_slot)` env field. Escape-unsafe by design (like Rust closures.rs:131) — safe ONLY because Rust gg rejects escaping mutating closures at borrow-check + the parity harness gates on the Rust oracle, so such programs are excluded. The self-host driver OMITS the borrow-check diagnostic (pre-existing, separately-filed gap), so for a Rust-REJECTED escaping mutating closure it would emit dangling-pointer C (vs stub garbage pre-Increment-2). Unreachable from valid Gorget, but defensive hardening = add an `is_move`/escape guard at the reclassify mirroring `closures.rs:102` (only ByMutRef a NON-escaping closure), OR wire the self-host borrow-check diagnostic. Belongs to the borrow-check-diagnostic-gap track.

## [test-infra nit, from Increment-2 review] 2 release-mode lib test failures
`cargo test --lib --release` fails `lir::validate::tests::assert_module_valid_*` (2 `#[should_panic]` tests that don't panic because `debug_assert!` is compiled out in release). PASS in debug (`cargo test --lib` = 1085/0, the CLAUDE.md-prescribed command). Pre-existing, benign, unrelated to any recent change. Fix: gate those `#[should_panic]` tests on `#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` or assert via a non-debug path. Low priority.

## [test-infra — RECURRING SWEEP FLAKE, file 2026-06-27] io_input.gg hangs in self_host_runtime under the parallel sweep (stdin-EOF)
`io_input.gg` is a stdin-READING fixture. In the `self_host_runtime` test (integration.rs:~152 spawn / ~:18413 assert) under the FULL parallel sweep (`--test-threads=4`), the child binary inherits a stdin that never EOFs → `read_line` blocks → times out at BOTH 30s AND 120s (NOT a load-timeout). It PASSES in isolation and with the test process's stdin `< /dev/null`. Every full round-close sweep hits this and reports a spurious 1-fixture failure on BOTH backends. **FIX: set the spawned child's stdin to `Stdio::null()`** (the run-binary helper around integration.rs:152) so stdin-reading fixtures get immediate EOF — or skip stdin-fixtures in `self_host_runtime`. Low-risk test-harness-only change; makes every future sweep clean. Until fixed, treat an **io_input-ONLY** sweep failure as GREEN (re-confirm: `cargo test --test integration --release self_host_runtime -- --exact self_host_runtime < /dev/null` → ok).

- **🔧 BRIEF-LESSON: executors that run gates in the BACKGROUND end their turn awaiting them** (needed a resume nudge twice — while-else, method-default). Brief future executors: "run ALL verification commands in the FOREGROUND; do NOT background a gate and end your turn awaiting it."
- **📄 DOCS — reconcile the eager-collection-HOF return-type notation** (surfaced 2026-06-28 pressure-testing the closure-HOF cluster): `docs/language-reference.md:2762-2763` lists `map`/`filter` with return-type column `Iterator(Collection, fn)`, but the OBSERVABLE + intended behavior (book `05-collections.md:537-539` `Vector[int] doubled = numbers.map(...)`, all corpus fixtures, the self-host's own `lower_expr.gg` usage, BOTH compilers via the eager inline-loop lowering) is EAGER-returns-`Vector[U]`/value. Eager direct collection HOFs are a first-class DOMINANT idiom (318 eager call-sites vs 167 `.iter()` chains in fixtures; the self-host compiler uses them) — confirmed NOT vestigial-pre-lazy-iterator. Fix the reference's return-type column to reflect the eager-collection result (`Vector[U]` for map/filter/sorted/…, `U` for fold/reduce) + a one-line note that `.iter()`-adapters are the separate LAZY path. Pure docs; low-pri.
- **🏛 DESIGN CONSIDERATION (filed 2026-06-28, from the eager-vs-lazy-HOF discussion) — a MONOMORPHIZED closure path alongside the erased one (Rust's `impl Fn` + `dyn Fn` split).** Today ALL Gorget closures are type-ERASED (16-byte `GorgetClosure`, indirect call). This blocks zero-cost CLOSURE-TAKING lazy adapters + zero-cost generic `Callable[T]` PARAMS. ✅ VERIFIED by emitted-C scout `ac233d9e` 2026-06-28 (precise): the adapter `next()` dispatch is ALREADY statically monomorphized (direct call, no vtable), so CLOSURE-FREE adapters (`take`/`skip`/`enumerate`) AND user-defined iterators (struct+`next()`, no closure) DO fuse to zero-cost AT `-O2`. It's specifically the CLOSURE `F` in `MapIter`/`FilterIter` (`lib/std/iter.gg:523`) that defeats fusion — `F` monomorphizes to the erased `GorgetClosure` EVEN FOR A NAMED FN (wrapped in an `__adapt_*` thunk), so the per-element call stays indirect (`((ret(*)(void*,arg))((void**)it)[0])((void**)it)[1], x)`) and cc keeps the `blr` even at `-O2` (no devirt, no fusion; chained `.map().filter()` stacks N indirect calls). ⚠ AND default cc is `-O0` (`-O2` only `--release`; the whole self-host path is `-O0`), so nothing fuses in a default build regardless — `-O2` is necessary-not-sufficient and only helps the closure-FREE cases. The reference-grade direction = a MONOMORPHIZED path for STATICALLY-KNOWN closures (unique-type-per-closure + generic-instantiate closure-taking fns/adapters → static, inlinable calls → fusion at `-O2`), KEPT ALONGSIDE the erased path for stored/heterogeneous/dynamic closures (`Vector[Callable]`/`Box[Callable]`/branch-returned — these REQUIRE erasure; the closure track added the GorgetClosure alias precisely for them). ⚠ NOT a prerequisite for the eager-HOF parity work — eager `v.map(f)` ALREADY inlines the closure body into the desugar loop (zero-cost, no GorgetClosure); monomorphization only buys the LAZY-iterator + generic-Callable cases. ⚠ TRADE-OFF: monomorphization = code-bloat + compile-time growth, which hits the SELF-HOST hardest (already ~130s self-compile, bootstrap-timeout-edge, closure-heavy) — and it only pays off at `-O2` (the `-O0` bootstrap won't fuse regardless). So it's necessary-not-sufficient (also need `-O2` + fusion-friendly emit). DEFERRED — evaluate (mono-path vs a compile-time iterator-fusion/deforestation pass — the alternative route to zero-cost lazy) WHEN the zero-cost-lazy-iterator / generic-`Callable[T]` perf project is funded. Foundational re-arch; owner decision before any work.
- **📄 DOCS BUG (found by the lazy-iter verify scout `ac233d9e`, 2026-06-28): `docs/book/05-collections.md:206` + `docs/book/19-stdlib.md:380` OVERCLAIM adapter-chain fusion.** They say adapter chains have "no boxing, no trait-object dispatch, the whole chain fuses at monomorphization." TRUE for the adapter STRUCT chain + `next()` dispatch (statically monomorphized) — but MISLEADING for the CLOSURE: a `.map(f)`/`.filter(f)` closure is type-erased (`GorgetClosure`) and its per-element invocation is an IRREDUCIBLE indirect call that does NOT fuse (verified in emitted C, survives `cc -O2`). Add a clarifying carve-out: the struct/dispatch fuses, but the closure call is an indirect dispatch per element (until the monomorphized-closure-path design item lands). Pure docs; low-pri.
- **🐛 DEEP (deferred) — self-host f-string BLANKET-inference blocked by the `span.start`-collision class.** [The nested-f-string synthetic-window collision is now FIXED — `071d71cd`, `parser.gg` shared-counter write-back + `deep_nest_fstring` guard.] But the `typecheck.gg` EFString guard `if fstring_interp_is_tag_check(fe): infer_expr_type(fe)` (`:1223`, helper `:1459`) STILL can't be retired to reference-grade unconditional interpolation inference. TWO measured root causes (scout `a7680a54`, 2026-07-01): **(a)** char predicates (`is_alpha`/`is_digit`/`is_alphanumeric`/`is_whitespace`/`is_upper`/`is_lower`/`is_ascii`/`is_hex_digit`) are absent from `infer_expr_type`'s bool arms (`infer.gg` EMethodCall) → mis-typed under broad inference (`char_methods` CC-FAIL `'Str' from '_Bool'`); cheap to add a char-predicate bool arm. **(b) THE BLOCKER** — an INLINE chained interp (`f"{name.trim().len()}"`): `infer_expr_type` recurses on `.trim()`, writes `String` at the chain's shared `span.start`; the outer `.len()` doesn't reliably override at `span.end` → `lookup_expr_gir_type` (lower_types.gg:551-562) reads String → garbage pointer (`string_fstring_stress` + the baseline `closure_fstring_capture`/`ecs_advanced` `187650…` WRONG-OUTPUT fixtures are this same class). Full retirement needs EVERY method-return arm in `infer_expr_type` to record `expr_link_types[span.end]` (like the tag-check/char arms) AND the lowerer's span.end oracle complete for ALL chains — broad, regression-prone. Reference-grade target = Rust's per-interp inference (`src/semantic/typecheck.rs` walker arms). Own scout→brief.
- **🐛 test_linked_list — 192-byte (3×64B) leak in `LinkedList__iter`→`gorget_array_push`, BOTH backends (Core #8).** `test_linked_list.gg:96-102` `iter()` builds a `Vector[int] ll_vals` moved into a `LinkedListIter`, whose backing array is never freed at iterator exhaustion (confirmed on the Rust-oracle `--sanitize` build: `LeakSanitizer: 192 byte(s) in 3 allocations`, 3 leaks = 3 `iter()` calls in main; stdout still byte-matches oracle). Reproduces on the Rust backend → a drop-insertion gap on an array field moved into an iterator struct, NOT a fixture-shape bug — fix in the COMPILER's drop tracking, in both. Independent of the round-28 bool-typing fix (touches no iterator codegen; revealed only because that fix made the fixture compile). Own scout.

## [closure follow-up, from lowerer-route scout — SIBLING of R2-completion] __result_unwrap_or_else drops the error payload
`__result_unwrap_or_else` (lir_codegen.gg:~4502) calls the closure as `((ret(*)(void*))…)(env)` — passing ONLY env, NOT the Error payload. A Result's `unwrap_or_else` closure takes the error payload (`(String e): len(e)`); reading `e` → garbage Str → SIGSEGV (test_result_all crashes here). `__option_unwrap_or_else` is correctly nullary (Option's closure takes no arg). My R2-completion fixture `combinator_unwrap_or_else_str` MASKED this — its `(int e)` closure ignores `e`. FIX (lir_codegen-only, +1 test_result_all): in `__result_unwrap_or_else`, read Error_0 (field idx 2; `src_err_c` already computed in the fn) and pass it: `((ret(*)(void*, src_err_c))…)(env, __err_pay)`. ALSO improve `combinator_unwrap_or_else_str` (or add a fixture) so the Result unwrap_or_else closure READS `e` (guard the defect). Sibling-class completion of R2.

## [PROMOTED TRACK — from Inc-6 scout] Ref[T]/MutRef[T] struct-field ctor-init dangling-pointer UAF (+1, mirrors Rust)
The struct/enum constructor field-init arg loop (lower_expr.gg:~5452-5453 + :5464-5465) unconditionally `op_consume(...CkFieldWrite())` every ctor arg with NO carve-out for BORROW-typed fields. A `Ref[T]`/`MutRef[T]` field (e.g. stdlib DictIter's `Ref[Dict] source`, lib/std/iter.gg:863) gets the owning clone → clones into a stack-local + stores `field = &stack-local` → DANGLING POINTER → DictIter reads garbage order_len → empty iteration. Canonical slice-view-to-stack-local UAF at the ctor boundary. FIX (prototyped, +1 closure_tuple_destructure, 0 regress/373): resolve the field tid at index ai (idiom of `_ucft` at :5301-5313 / `lookup_ctor_field_type` for variants), match GtPtr/GtMutPtr → push `OpBorrow(arg_local)` instead of `op_consume(CkFieldWrite())`. ~28 lines, typed, mirrors Rust `lower_struct_literal` field_is_ptr→continue (src/ir/lowering/exprs/mod.rs:1994-2001). LANDING NOW.

## ====== REFERENCE-GRADE REJECTION TRACK (owner-asked 2026-06-27; audit @ 65c2cdc0) ======
⚠⚠ OWNER STEER 2026-06-27 (TWO-PHASE): **PHASE 1 (NOW, until 100% parity)** = PARITY is PRIMARY; do bounded single-node rejections OPPORTUNISTICALLY only (FP-enumerable, parity-neutral; they seed the negative corpus). **PHASE 2 (AFTER 100% parity)** = build the GENERAL type-enforcement pass (explicit-VarDecl type-mismatch → un-ignores `self_host_check_rejects_illtyped`; #6 TYPE-mismatch ~42-`self.error`-site migration; #8 borrow-check port). DEFERRED-NOT-DECLINED — the enforcement pass is worth doing, just AFTER parity (it's parity-orthogonal + reads more-trustworthy types once inference is maximally correct + the dominant risk is FP). So in PHASE 1: do NOT start #6/#7/#8 or the VarDecl-mismatch as a track; only land a rejection if it's bounded + FP-enumerable.
SELF-HOST IS TOO PERMISSIVE: Rust gg rejects 116 fixtures, self-host ACCEPTS 109 (emits C). Self-host typecheck is an INFERENCE pass (~6 control-flow diagnostics only), NO general type/arg/trait/const enforcement, NO borrow/move/safety pass. Parity-NEUTRAL (rejected fixtures are RUST-REJECTED, excluded from denom) — pure correctness wins per invariant #8.
TEST MECHANISM (exists, under-used): `check_gg_fails` (integration.rs:6486) = Rust rejects; `self_host_driver_rejects_invalid_program` (integration.rs:16510, runs driver `--lir-c`, asserts non-zero exit + codespan + EMPTY stdout) = self-host rejects. Clone the latter per gap. (The hole: c_emit_comparison:15182 + self_host_runtime_diff:17897 `return RustRejected` without running the self-host.)
★ EVERY rejection MUST gate on: 1208-Rust-accepted-fixture FP sweep (0 false positives) + `self_host_bootstrap_fixed_point` (a new reject that trips the self-host's OWN 667K-line source breaks bootstrap) + `type_comparison`.
WRITE SITE: self_host_typechecker/typecheck.gg (SYMLINKED into self_host_lowerer → affects build/check + type_comparison + bootstrap).
LANDING ORDER (most-bounded first; items 1/2/3a are in DONE.md):
3b. wrong_arg_count / wrong_field_count (signature via infer_expr_type/scope) — ⚠ wrong_arg_count is ENTANGLED with the filed 🐛 Rust-gg method-default-arg-fill defect (Rust mis-counts method calls that omit a defaulted arg); resolve that defect FIRST, or scope 3b to free-function arg-count only.
4. CONVERT/CAST single-node form-rejections (str()/cast-name calls, deref-non-box, string-index-assign, out-of-range).
5. non_exhaustive_match (MODERATE — collect-all-variants + diff; else/_ wildcard + qualified-vs-bare must be exact).
6. TYPE-mismatch family ~19 (BIGGER behavioral decision — typecheck deliberately non-enforcing; respect the 42 type_comparison supersets; FP-sweep each).
7. const_assign / await_outside_async — parser DISCARDS local-`const` (parser.gg:3546) + `async` (:3113) flags → thread flag through parser+AST FIRST, then a bounded check.
8. DEEP BORROW-CHECK port ~44 (MOVE ~17/BORROW ~8/ARENA ~12/CLOSURE-CAPTURE ~5/CLOSURE-ESCAPE ~2): port Rust `BorrowChecker` (safety/mod.rs:241, ~2000 lines: var_states move-dataflow, var_origins lifetime, arena_depth, loop_depth, struct-field-ref flags, CoW carve-outs). HIGH FP risk (carve-outs: loop-local re-create, `x=f(!x)` rebind waiver, CoW accept-live-at-ctor, imported-module skip). Multi-session sub-project. ## [rejection follow-up, invariant #8 — from Rejection-#1 review] positional-after-named on METHOD calls accepted by BOTH compilers
Rust gg's `check_named_args_and_defaults` (PositionalAfterNamed) is invoked at ONLY the free-function ECall path (typecheck.rs:1569), NOT for method calls — Rust gg ACCEPTS `s.compute(a=1, 2)` (exit 0), and so does the self-host. So Rejection #1 (self-host ECall, not EMethodCall) faithfully mirrors Rust's CURRENT scope. But per invariant #8 this is a ≥2-bug defect BOTH compilers share: reject positional-after-named on METHOD calls in Rust gg (extend the check to the EMethodCall path) AND the self-host (add the same walk to the EMethodCall typecheck arm) + a negative fixture asserting both reject. Separate follow-up.

## ====== 🏛 DEFINE GORGET — THE ACTIVE FLAGSHIP TRACK (owner GO 2026-07-05) ======
**The executable definition of Gorget's semantics.** ALL state, process, and next actions live in `docs/plans/define-gorget/` — read `HANDOVER.md` FIRST (it has the fresh-session read order), then `decisions.md` (owner decisions D1–D8 + standing directives + the open decision queue), `rfc-ggc-ggdef.md` (APPROVED architecture), `phase0-brief.md` (current work item: 3 increments A/B/C, status header tracks review passes + executor launches). Owner directives: token-constrained — run agents with `model: "opus"`; briefs are written to be Opus-executable, and an Opus stall is a BRIEF defect to fix. Prerequisite bug for D4 spectests: the collection-element custom-Drop HIGH entry below (separate parallel track, src/ir zone). The D2/D4/D5/D6 implementation tracks (both compilers) are phase-1 items — do NOT start them before phase 0 lands.
- **[MEDIUM — after phase 2's context pack] 🤖 LLM-correctness KPI (owner directive 2026-07-05, recorded in decisions.md):** a measured, ratcheted "LLM zero-shot correctness" benchmark — fixed task suite, model given ONLY the generated semantic context pack, score compiles-first-try + runs-correct rates; wire as a diagnostic-always-pass report with a monotone floor (the house ratchet pattern). A first-class language-quality metric aimed at the LLM audience; no other language measures this.

### Trap-normalization (D11) T2a-rust follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2a-rust executor)
- **[MEDIUM — T1-zone] `spectests/run/trap_shift.gg` conformance fixture (BLOCKED on ggdef modeling shift).** T2a-rust made an out-of-range shift (`x << 64`) trap `T_Overflow` + exit 101 on BOTH Rust backends (C already trapped; the missing LLVM shift-range check was ADDED, owner ruling 2026-07-10). There is currently an integration test pinning both-backend agreement (`tests/integration.rs` shift test) but NO conformance fixture, because ggdef's evaluator (`spec/ggdef/src/eval.rs`) does not yet MODEL a shift operator / its out-of-range trap. To add `trap_shift.gg` (ggdef-adjudicated), first extend ggdef to evaluate `<<`/`>>` and Trap `Overflow` when the count is out of range, then generate the fixture (crosses into T1's zone + shifts MIN_FIXTURES/floors). Keep the closed registry unchanged (shift → existing `T_Overflow`, no new variant).
- **[MEDIUM — T1-zone] `spectests/run/trap_assert_cmp.gg` conformance fixture for the MESSAGE-LESS comparison assert (`assert a == b`).** T2a-rust rerouted BOTH assert forms to `trap[T_AssertFailed]` + exit 101: the message form (via `gorget_trap`) AND the message-less comparison form (via `gorget_assert_fail_values`, now a 4-arg sig carrying the `T_AssertFailed` code — approach (a)). The existing `trap_assert.gg` exercises the MESSAGE form only; the message-less form is currently pinned by integration tests (`assert_fails`, `test_should_panic`, `test_failure`) but not by a conformance fixture. ggdef already models `Stmt::Assert`, so it's generatable — but adding a spectests fixture crosses into T1's zone + shifts MIN_FIXTURES/floors, so do it as a separate small T1 follow-up.
- **[MEDIUM — structural guard] The LLVM `block_exit_labels` twin pre-pass silently drifts from the emit.** `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs:3008` reconstructs each block's exit label (for phi predecessors) by RE-RUNNING the emit's `trap_counter`/block-splitting logic in a SEPARATE match — a hand-maintained twin. T2a-rust's new Shl/Shr shift range-check (which splits the block + bumps `trap_counter`) had to be mirrored there by hand; forgetting it produced `llc: use of undefined value '%ov.*.ok'` that ONLY the self-host bootstrap caught (all trap fixtures + spec_conformance passed). This is a latent class: any future block-splitting `Inst` variant added to `emit_inst` without a matching arm in `block_exit_labels` silently miscompiles under a phi. Two options: (a) a `tests/lints.rs` guard asserting the two match-arm sets agree (the `Inst` variants that bump the counter must be identical in both), or (b) the real fix — have `emit_inst` RECORD each block's exit label into a map as it emits (single source of truth), deleting the twin entirely. Prefer (b). **⚠ NOW ≥2 CONFIRMED INSTANCES + a latent sibling (2026-07-10 — escalate off low-pri):** (1) T2a-rust's Shl/Shr shift check (fixed). (2) **T2b's `gorget_array_get_at` early-return** (`llvm/mod.rs:~4647`) skipped the generic-CallExtern `*trap_counter += 1` the pre-pass counts → 12 conformance fixtures BUILD-FAIL on the LLVM lane (`arr[i]` before an overflow-checked op, e.g. `sum += a[i]` in a loop); output-review caught it, fixed with a manual bump (`feee30d3`). (3) **LATENT, pre-existing:** the newtype-constructor early-return directly below (`llvm/mod.rs:~4658`) ALSO returns without bumping while the pre-pass counts it — builds+runs correctly TODAY but is the same class, one early-return away from a miscompile. (4) **LATENT, from the xhigh review 2026-07-10 (CONFIRMED mechanism):** the `gorget_trap` CallExtern early-return (`llvm/mod.rs:~4625`) bumps `trap_counter` 0/1/2× (once per GorgetString-typed arg via `marshal_cstr`) while the pre-pass counts every CallExtern as exactly 1 — masked ONLY because every current producer ends its block with `Term::Unreachable`; any future mid-block `gorget_trap` emitter (warn-then-continue trap, a pass appending a drop after the call) desyncs the `ov.*.ok` labels → `llc: use of undefined value`. Fix (b) retires ALL of these (incl. the two manual bumps). **⚠ FIX-(b) MEASURED (R-C scout 2026-07-10): NOT drop-in.** The pre-pass is 227 lines, single consumer (`mod.rs:3297`); (b)'s mechanics are ~25 lines (phi placeholders + record `current_label` per block + post-loop patch) MINUS the 227 — but correctness INVERTS to "every label-creating site must update `current_label`", and at least one already violates it: `gorget_str_clear` creates `scl.done` labels and returns WITHOUT updating (`mod.rs:5356-5361`; the pre-pass covers it at `:3238`). ~44 label-writing sites need that audit; the FULL LLVM sweep is the only honest gate. Own track, bootstrap-gated; instance count when it runs: 2 fixed + 2 latent + the R-C guard's mirrored arm to retire.
- **[LOW — cosmetic, not conformance-compared] Message-form `assert` trap line renders `at <unknown>:0:0`.** T2a-rust reroutes assert-with-message to `gorget_trap(T_AssertFailed, msg)` → `gorget_trap_at` threading the emit-site span (like `panic`). The code + exit 101 are correct and BYTE-IDENTICAL across C/LLVM, but the source LOCATION on the assert trap line resolves to `<unknown>:0:0` (Q-D wanted a real span). Root cause is a PRE-EXISTING GIR→LIR span-propagation gap for these branch-target trap blocks — the old `gorget_panic` assert emitted `<unknown>:0:0` too, and `condition.span` IS a real byte range (verified), yet the LIR instruction carries no span (both `call_extern` and `call_void` reproduce it, so it is NOT the Call/CallExtern distinction). Conformance does NOT compare the location (impl-defined, Q1), so this is cosmetic. Fix belongs in the GIR→LIR span-map plumbing for branch-target blocks, not trap normalization.

- **🆕 [MEDIUM — same staleness family as R-A's Fix 1, from R-A pass-1 review 2026-07-10] `ast_type_to_resolved` binds stale Import placeholders in TYPE positions.** It uses value-first `lookup` (`src/semantic/types.rs:403`) and ACCEPTS `DefKind::Import` (`:413`) — so a cross-module imported trait in a type position binds the stale placeholder DefId, and e.g. `Box[ImportedTrait]`'s trait-object detection (`:424-433`, requires `kind == DefKind::Trait` at `:428`) silently fails cross-module. Same import-placeholder-staleness family as the R-A trait-registry keying fix, but a DIFFERENT read path — needs its own scout (what breaks: trait objects? equip resolution? generic bounds?) + fixtures. Own track.
- **🆕 [MEDIUM — typed-metadata smell, from the R-C scout 2026-07-10] The LIR extern decl hardcodes an `i64` return for the monomorphized `Result__T__E__unwrap_error` mangles** — the combinator route's return type should come from typed metadata (the Result's error-payload type), not a default; the R-C guard works around it at the LLVM emit (val_types dst = field-2 payload) and the C twin has its own resolution. Root fix = type the extern decl at the LIR producer (rule 2), retiring both backend-side resolutions. Pairs with the `Inst::CallExtern` typed-routing-flag cleanup in the review-residuals (f) bundle.
- **🆕 [LOW — T1-zone, from the R-C scout 2026-07-10] Combinator-route spectest twin for `trap_unwrap_error_on_ok`** — the existing conformance fixture exercises only the Tier-2a LIR route; the combinator route (static/global receiver, `Result__T__E__unwrap_error` mangle) is pinned only by the new integration fixtures. A spectests twin (ggdef-generated, static receiver shape) makes the route conformance-compared on all lanes. Crosses MIN_FIXTURES/floors → separate small T1 follow-up (same shape as the trap_shift/trap_assert_cmp entries).

### D23-throws smith follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T3b executor)
- **[MEDIUM] 🎲 T3c — a POSITIVE throws DIFFERENTIAL smith tier.** T3b (LANDED, see DONE.md) is the NEGATIVE/rejection tier: `GG_SMITH_TIER=1` generates programs well-formed EXCEPT for ONE unhandled `throws` call and asserts production REJECTS with `E_UnhandledThrows` (an inverted, CHECK-ONLY oracle — `classify_throws` in `tests/smith/main.rs`, `program_throws_positions` in `tests/smith/generator.rs`). T3c is the complement and is arguably HIGHER-value: generate programs with HANDLED throws (auto-propagate inside a `throws` fn, `catch`/`rethrow`, `Result[T,E]` capture, the §10.3 capture forms) that COMPILE, then run the FULL backend differential (production `gg check`→C build/run, ggdef in-process, self-host, LLVM) exactly like tier 0. ggdef already MODELS handled throws (`Propagate`, §10.3 capture, `spec/ggdef/src/elaborate/mod.rs`), so it can adjudicate — this would catch a backend MISCOMPILING the throws→`Result` desugar at RUNTIME (a class the negative tier structurally cannot reach, since those programs never compile). It is a NEW tier number (not `TIER_THROWS=1`) reusing tier 0's differential `classify` path, so no new oracle shape — the work is the generator grammar (a `throws`-helper set + the handling forms) plus a tier dispatch arm. Own scout→brief→≥3 reviews before launch (the generator MUST emit programs that are well-formed AND actually handle the throws — the false-positive discipline from T3b's scout applies in mirror image).
- **[LOW] 🎲 T3b equip-method receiver-position extension (deferred from T3b).** T3b's generator ships the free-fn `risky()` form only; the `equip`-method receiver position (`s.m()` where `int m(self) throws String:`) was OMITTED because it needs a synthesized `struct S` + in-scope instance + `equip S:` block to be well-formed, and the scout found the false-positive trap that a bare `int risky(self) throws String:` WITHOUT `equip` is ACCEPTED (`self` parses as a plain param) — a mis-resolving shape would mis-fire the inverted oracle as a spurious slip. T3a's `d23_unhandled_method{,_traitdefault,_xmod}` fixtures already cover the method-reject, so this is incremental hardening, not a gap. If added: spell out + verify the struct+instance+equip scaffolding produces a well-formed-except-for-the-one-throws program before wiring it as a new position in `emit_throws_position`.
### Trap-normalization (D11) T2a-selfhost follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2a-selfhost executor)
- **[MEDIUM — self-host-shift-parity, Core-#8] The self-host emits an UNGUARDED out-of-range shift — the lone backend with silent UB on `x << 64`.** T2a-rust made an out-of-range shift trap `T_Overflow` + exit 101 on BOTH Rust backends (C already trapped; the LLVM shift-range check was added, owner ruling 2026-07-10). The self-host lowerer does NOT guard shift at all: `tests/fixtures/self_host_lowerer/lir_codegen.gg:4536` (IShl) / `:4539` (IShr) emit raw `v = lhs << rhs;` / `>> ` with no range check, so a self-host-compiled `x << 64` is silent UB where both Rust backends now trap. This is PRE-EXISTING (the self-host never guarded shift) and NOT in the conformance corpus (ggdef does not model shift yet — see the T2a-rust `trap_shift.gg` item above), so it does NOT affect the 7-fixture flip and it is NOT a "reroute an existing guard" (there is no guard to reroute — it needs a NEW one). Fix: add the self-host shift-range guard emitting `gorget_trap("T_Overflow", "shift out of range")` (mirroring the Rust C emit `src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:2649/2656`: `if ((uint64_t)rhs >= (uint64_t)(sizeof(ct)*8)) { gorget_trap(...); }`) + a self-host-lane shift test, so all three backends agree. Keep the closed registry unchanged (shift → existing `T_Overflow`, no new variant).

### Trap-normalization (D11) T2b follow-ups (filed 2026-07-10 by the T2b executor)
- **[LOW — reference-grade span plumbing; not conformance-compared] The RARER bounds-class sites still trap `trap[T_Bounds] at <unknown>:0:0` (span-less) — thread real `file:line:col` into them, mirroring the flagship `gorget_array_get_at`.** T2b gave the flagship Vector element READ (`v[i]`) a REAL source location via `gorget_array_get_at(arr, idx, code, file, line, col)` + an emit-boundary reroute on BOTH Rust backends (`src/backend/c_lir/emit_call_extern.rs`, `src/backend/llvm/mod.rs`), but the rest of the index/bounds class stays SPAN-LESS (owner ruling: flagship-only in T2b): the runtime helpers `gorget_array_set/remove/insert/swap/swap_remove/slice`, `gorget_str_index/slice/byte_slice/byte_at`, `gorget_shared_array_get/set`, and the ~16 `gorget_bytes_*` offset checks all call the span-less `gorget_trap(GG_T_BOUNDS, detail)` → `trap[T_Bounds]: … at <unknown>:0:0` + exit 101 (correct code + exit; only the LOCATION is `<unknown>`). Conformance does NOT compare the location (impl-defined, D11 Q1), so this is reference-grade polish, not a correctness gap. Fix = per-site `_at` variants (arr/idx/…, code, file, line, col) + an emit-boundary reroute for each of the `v[i]=x` write / `.remove` / `.insert` / `.slice` / `str[i]` / `shared[i]` / bytes-offset call sites, exactly as `gorget_array_get_at` did for the read. This is the same per-runtime-fn span-plumbing as the broader runtime-side item (see the "Runtime-side panic locations (~50+ sites)" entry) — could fold into it. Pairs with self-host span-threading (`<unknown>:0:0` there too, existing LOW entry).

### Self-host reject-diagnostic-rendering alignment (filed 2026-07-15; split off from the ggdef elab∘eval landing, owner-confirmed — the COMMITTED next track that completes four-lane-green)
  - **🆕🐛 [HIGH — DEFINITION-INTEGRITY (oracle under-rejection), from the coarse-kind scout 2026-07-16] ggdef ACCEPTS (exit 0) programs BOTH production compilers reject on the range/signature/main-throws axes — and its `reject_code` verdict axis is MAY-MOVE-ONLY (`spec/ggdef/src/lib.rs:148`).** Measured: `ggdef run` on the real coarse-kind reject corpus yields NO `reject: E_X` verdict for ANY of them — buckets: FrontendError-SKIP (deref/default-op/positional/throw), eval-IllFormed-codeless (string-index/break/continue), and outright ACCEPT (value-range / required-after-default / main-throws — the oracle blesses ill-formed programs, same under-rejection class as the ConsumeCallable bug). Per the ratified `verdict = elaborate ∘ eval` boundary (2026-07-16): **ggdef-elaborate must model every ratified static rejection within its subset** — extend elaborate beyond may-move to the type/control-flow/signature axes (each E_ code as a typed rule, static walk, no execution), which THEN unblocks the coarse-kind four-lane spectests migration (floors move only after this). Sequencing: elaborate-extension track AFTER the coarse-kind split lands (the split is floor-neutral and independent). Own scout→brief→gauntlet (Rust, spec/ggdef/, not bootstrap-gated).
  - **🆕 [LOW — registry-prose completeness, from the self-host reject-diagnostic pass-1 review 2026-07-16] `E_LocalBorrowBind` is emitted by BOTH production (`src/semantic/errors.rs:715`) and (post-reject-diagnostic-landing) the self-host, but is ABSENT from the registry-prose table `spec/prose/diagnostic-codes.md` (which the briefs call "the source of truth").** Add the missing `E_LocalBorrowBind` row so the registry is actually complete. Zero conformance impact today (no d10a spectest exists), pure doc completeness. Trivial — fold into the next diagnostic-codes doc touch.

### Toolchain exit-code scheme follow-ups (filed 2026-07-15 — production polish, surfaced by the exit-code research backing the ggdef elab∘eval verdict-triple; scheme RATIFIED Option A in decisions.md: 0 success / 1 static-rejection / 2 usage / 101 trap+ICE / 103 ggdef-only fuel)
- **[LOW — production `gg`, consensus polish] Usage/CLI errors collapse into exit 1 instead of 2.** Per the ratified scheme, genuine usage errors (bad flags, missing file, misinvocation) should exit **2** (GNU/argparse consensus), distinct from a *source* static-rejection (exit 1). Production `gg` currently exits 1 at ~50 `process::exit(1)` sites in `src/main.rs`, conflating "you invoked me wrong" with "your source is wrong." Audit those sites, split genuine usage errors to 2, leave parse/semantic/build failures at 1. Not conformance-compared (ggdef's own usage=2 already correct); reference-grade legibility polish. Pairs with the ggdef `EXIT_USAGE=2` that already holds.
- **[LOW — production `gg`, Core-#8 flavor] Internal runtime panics exit 1, colliding with the compile-error code.** `gorget_panic_at` (`src/backend/c/runtime/panic_normal.c:5`) handles *internal* runtime failures (OOM, closed-channel send, file-open error) and exits **1** — numerically colliding with the static-rejection/compile-error code, so a shell script can't distinguish "your program failed to compile" from "your program ran and hit an internal panic." Per the ratified taxonomy these are *runtime* failures and should either be traps (exit 101, via `gorget_trap_at`) or get their own distinct code. Decide which (are these D11 catchable traps or a separate ICE-adjacent class?), then reroute + a both-backend test. Distinct from the user-facing `gorget_trap_at`=101 path which is already correct.

## ====== STRATEGIC ASSESSMENT RECOMMENDATIONS (owner-requested 2026-07-05; from the full docs+repo three-pillars review) ======

Each item below gets its own scout→brief→≥3 reviews before launch.

- **[MEDIUM] 🔔 DeadBareParamWrite lint FOLLOW-UPS (v1 LANDED 2026-07-05, see DONE.md — the follow-ups below are the remaining work).** v1 warns on bare-param CoW writes with no later read; zero FPs across 1479 fixtures + lib + self-host; 25 fixtures + 16 unit tests pin the semantics. Remaining: (a) **docs/book mention** — the warning should appear in book ch.11/12's bare-vs-`&` discussion and the README static-analysis bullet (it's a learnability feature; undocumented warnings under-deliver); (b) **`f(&p)`-arg write detection** — v1 doesn't treat passing `&p` to a mutating callee as a write; gate on §3.8 purity's `MutatesArgs` when threading it is cheap; (c) **include `self`** once the plain-`self` write-through bug (HIGH entry above) is resolved; (d) two reviewer nits: clear `deadwrite_stmt_expr_start` in the check_function reset block (consistency; provably balanced today), and the executor-brief archive noted `GG_BUILD_TIMEOUT_SECS` (not `GG_TEST_TIMEOUT_SECS`) governs the self-host-driver emit step in the cow slice.
- **[MEDIUM] 🎲 gorget-smith FOLLOW-UPS (tier-0 LANDED 2026-07-05, see DONE.md; round 1 found 2 HIGH bugs — entries above).** Run it: `GG_SMITH_SEEDS=1..500 cargo test --test smith -- --nocapture` (always-pass diagnostic; `GG_SMITH_MINIMIZE=<seed>` to ddmin a finding; `GG_SMITH_SKIP_LLVM=1` to skip the expensive lane). Remaining: (a) **tier-1 grammar** — the devbook/11 mutation-route table shapes (`.get().unwrap()` views, string index/slice views, nested vectors, struct resource fields, W3a-d hooks) — this is where the ASan-blind class lives and where sustained yield comes from as tier-0 bugs get fixed; (b) **CI wiring** — a bounded post-merge job (N seeds) mirroring runtime_diff's role; (c) reviewer nits: SH-lane stdout diff uses `trim_end()` while LLVM lane compares exact (stricter, no signal lost — add a comment or unify); minimizing a CRASH seed pays the full SH+LLVM pipeline per candidate (slow; use SKIP_LLVM); STR Bare args can generate `f(s1, !s1)` (borrow+move same string, would classify GEN-INVALID if check rejects — arguably a legitimate probe shape, not observed in 320 seeds); (d) as tier-0 findings get FIXED, delete the corresponding excluded-shapes entries so the generator re-covers them. (`gg sim` oracle status: NOT maintained per owner 2026-07-05; measured unable to run the CoW shapes — 7/11 SIMFAIL + 1 silent wrong-output on an 11-fixture probe. The justifying feature for any sim catch-up is "sim runs the smith divergence corpus under --ub-checks"; that corpus is the acceptance test.)
- **[HIGH — BUG; independently found 2026-07-05 by the dead-write-lint scout, MERGES the round-35 MEDIUM Core-#8 entry (filed 2026-07-03)] 🐛 Plain-`self` mutation WRITES THROUGH to the caller — contradicts the documented "self = immutable borrow" AND §3.1's uniform bare-binding rule.** Probe: `void poke(self): self.log.push(1)` compiles silently and mutates the caller's struct (probe printed 2, not 1 — write-through, no CoW materialize, no rejection). Known facts from the round-35 filing: affects BOTH Rust and self-host identically (matches the Rust oracle); `self` is `LoOwned` so round-35 T4's gate doesn't fire on it. Per language-design §4.5 `self` is an immutable borrow and per §3.1 a write through a bare binding must materialize — so either fix BOTH compilers (reject or materialize) OR make an explicit language-design decision that plain-`self` write-through is intended and fix the DOCS (§4.5, book ch.11, MEMORY notes) — the contradiction is the defect either way (invariant-#8 flavor). Probe at /tmp/recover_deadwrite/scout_deadwrite_probes/self_plain_mut.gg. NOTE: this is why `self` is excluded from the new dead-write lint — revisit including `self` once resolved. Own scout→brief→reviews; the scout's FIRST job is the design question (owner call).
- **[HIGH — BUG, found by dead-write-brief review pass 2, 2026-07-05] 🐛 Unknown method on `Option[T]` passes `gg check`, dies at LINK time.** One-liner: `p.pop().to_str()` (no `to_str` on Option) → `gg check` exit 0, `gg build` fails with `undefined reference to Option__int64_t__to_str`. Check-accepts + build-explodes = invariant-#8 class: the method-existence check on builtin generic enums (Option, likely Result — probe both) is missing at typecheck. Likely belongs to the REFERENCE-GRADE REJECTION TRACK's "TYPE-mismatch family / typecheck deliberately non-enforcing" cluster (this file, landing-order item 6) — triage whether it's that family or a separate method-resolution hole before fixing; needs the standard FP sweep + a negative fixture asserting BOTH compilers reject. Repro probe: `void handle(Vector[int] p): p.pop().to_str()`.
- **[HIGH — PRODUCTION BUG, found by B1 output-review probing 2026-07-06] 🐛 Struct/enum-CTOR named args bind POSITIONALLY in Rust gg — silent wrong values.** `Point(y=200, x=100)` → production x=200,y=100; correct (and ggdef) x=100,y=200. DONE.md 2026-06-27 wired free-fn/method named-reorder but left "ctor paths ... verified untouched" — the gap was never filed. Mirror-image note: ggdef had the complementary bug on CALL-side named args (silently positional) — now rejected at elaboration pending the B2 reorder. Fix production ctor paths + negative/positive fixtures; both-compiler check (self-host ctor named-arg behavior UNVERIFIED — probe in the same pass). Own scout→brief→reviews.
- **[HIGH — ggdef phase-1 MUST, from B2 output-review 2026-07-06] 🐛 ggdef transitive drop execution: `run_custom_drop` skips droppable FIELDS and COLLECTION ELEMENTS of the dropped value** — nested Drop types miss the inner drop; `Vector[R]`-of-Drop misses all element drops (verified vs correct production, which now handles both post-elemdrop). RFC §2.2 makes drop count/order NORMATIVE, and drop-count spectests (phase 1) cannot gate implementations until the definition itself is transitively correct. Not exercised by the phase-0 corpus (gate legitimately green); disclosed in spec/ggdef/reports/increment_b2.md CORRECTION. Also from the same review: D4 position-6 plain-`self`-write detection needs method-body write analysis (the `&self` sibling was closed post-review with pinning tests); position-5 capture check should consolidate into the centralized helper; CLI should exit non-zero on elaboration errors (provisional codes); `-- gen` should skip/error on non-`run`-mode fixtures (today it would clobber a static-error expect block — harmless while error tiers are empty). One phase-1 track.
- **[HIGH — ggdef native-recursion SIGABRT; separate track split off from the P1-A throw-drop fix 2026-07-06] 🐛 ggdef's big-step eval recurses on the NATIVE stack, so a deeply-recursive fixture SIGABRTs (stack overflow) instead of reporting `FuelExhausted`.** `stack_guard_deep_recursion.gg` blows the real stack — fuel bounds STEP count, not eval/elaborate DEPTH; RFC §2.2's "ggdef is total via fuel and never exhausts a real stack" is currently FALSE. Fix = add a recursion-depth guard (tied to fuel or an explicit depth counter) → `FuelExhausted`/`Trap`, OR soften the RFC §2.2 text and keep this fixture in the RFC-C11 implementation-defined exclusion set (owner call). The two promoted P1-A gates (`spec/ggdef/tests/coverage_histogram.rs`, `converter_agreement.rs`) currently EXCLUDE `stack_guard_deep_recursion.gg` by name + run in a 2 GiB-stack thread to avoid the crash; that exclusion is the workaround this fix retires. (The bundled throw-drop half of this entry LANDED — see DONE.md 2026-07-06.)
- **[HIGH — BOTH-COMPILER rejection gap, surfaced by the scout's prototype smith ggdef-lane 2026-07-06; the A29 exclusivity hole made concrete] 🐛 `gg check` ACCEPTS same-call borrow+move aliasing — `f(w0, r1, !r1)` (r1 read as a borrow arg AND moved in the same call) passes check (exit 0); ggdef flags read-of-moved.** Per RFC §2.3 + A29 (decisions.md), same-call aliasing rejection is a PREMISE of D1's refinement claim — this is invariant-#8 (should-be-rejected, both compilers accept). Repro seeds 110/142/195 (minimized: /tmp/recover_p1infra/scout_seed110.gg). Belongs to the A29 consolidation work; file the rejection in both compilers + negative fixtures. Own scout→brief→reviews (may fold into the A29 static-semantics prose track). **ADDENDUM (aliasing decision scout 2026-07-06, full sigil-pair matrix at /tmp/recover_scout_aliasing/): the check is also NAME-EXACT, not PLACE-overlap — `f(b.data, &b)` (projection of the borrowed root read in the same call) is ACCEPTED (probe q3f2). The class fix keys on place overlap (root + projection prefix) across all {bare,&,!}² pairs where ≥1 writes/moves — closes the `f(v,!v)` arm AND the projection dimension in one rule (Core #4). Reference §9.4 table (language-reference.md:2297-2301) needs the missing arms too.**

Order is mandatory 1→2→3; float spectests migrate only after all three.
- **[MEDIUM — ggdef, owner-question-driven 2026-07-06] 🔍 HOST-INHERITANCE AUDIT: enumerate and PIN every place spec/ggdef's eval delegates to Rust behavior** — the D9 class generalized (ggdef's float printing WAS Rust's Display until D9 pinned it). Sweep eval.rs for semantic delegation to the host language: integer division/modulo SIGN conventions, shift semantics, float NaN handling + comparisons + casts, string/Unicode ops, container iteration order, sort stability, hash behavior, format conversions. Each becomes an explicit spec rule (prose + unit test) or a documented Nondet — never an implicit inheritance. Deliverable: an audit table in spec/prose (rule | pinned-by | test). Rationale vs multi-language ggdef reimplementation: the definition must stay SINGULAR (Knight-Leveson correlated-error result; Wasm/SML/MiniRust precedent — one definitional artifact); redundancy comes from prose-parity review, this audit, the phase-2 Lean mechanization (the principled second implementation), and eventually a post-1.0 Gorget port of ggdef as a confirmation artifact. Fits phase 1-2; small.

Missing: (a) the free macro benchmark — self-host compiler compile-time + peak RSS on a fixed input, Gorget-built vs Rust gg (commands, not numbers, into the handover); (b) a small cross-language macro set (JSON parse, HTTP-server throughput, benchmarks-game-style workloads) vs C, Rust, Go, Python; (c) `--clones=stats` tracked per benchmark so CoW-placement quality is a measured, regression-guarded number. Delete the rows. Separately, reframe the pillar claim in README/design docs: the book's own content (~60 keywords, ~40 special types, ~30 traits, ownership+concurrency chapters with no Python analog) does not support "easier than Python" — the defensible and still-compelling pitch is "Python-familiar surface, dramatically gentler than Rust, pay-as-you-go depth". Owner sign-off on wording.
- **[LOW] 🗺 Competitive positioning doc vs the real neighbors.** README compares only Rust/Go/Python. The nearest competitors for "Python-easy + fast + safe" are Nim (indentation syntax, compiles to C, no lifetime annotations, ARC/ORC), Swift (the CoW-value-semantics precedent), Mojo, and Hylo (mutable value semantics). Write a short "why Gorget over Nim/Swift/Mojo" section (differentiators: zero-annotation ownership + fully-lazy CoW for user types, batteries, one toolchain) — needed before any public launch; also sharpens internal design decisions.
- **[LOW] 🐞 DWARF line-table debug info (gdb/lldb stepping).** `Backend.debug_info: bool` exists but is hardwired `false` (`src/backend/c_lir/mod.rs:~3438`). The C backend could get source-line stepping nearly free via `#line` directives mapping emitted C back to `.gg` source; LLVM needs `!dbg` metadata. Distinct from (and cheaper than) the existing Low entry on `caller_location()`/multi-frame backtraces. Unblocks "can I actually debug a Gorget program" — table stakes before 1.0, after LSP.
- **[MEDIUM — OWNER DECISION] 🔮 `gg sim` disposition (filed 2026-07-05 from owner discussion; owner: sim duplicates backend effort, "architecture has not been designed properly yet", deprioritized, deletion considered).** Facts (verified 2026-07-05): sim consumes **GIR** (`sim::interpret(module: &crate::ir::Module)`, `src/sim/mod.rs:31`) while real backends consume **BIR** (`Backend::generate(&BirModule)`, `src/backend/mod.rs:382`) — so sim forks ABOVE LIR/BIR and must re-derive everything the shared pipeline does below GIR (drop elaboration, value-type passes, lazy-CoW materialization — which is structurally WHY it now gives wrong CoW answers, see the fuzzing item's probe). It also re-implements the runtime in parallel (`dispatch.rs` 6,278 lines + `runtime.rs` 2,358 + its own `crypto.rs` 299). NOT in CI; IS user-facing documented (`README.md` CLI table, `docs/book/appendix-cli.md:184-200` incl. `--ub-checks`). **Immediate sub-item regardless of disposition:** a shipped, documented command that silently prints wrong output on CoW programs (invariant-#8 flavor) — either emit a loud experimental/unmaintained warning on `gg sim` startup or pull it from README/book until a decision. **Disposition options:** (a) DELETE (~10.4K lines + test_sim.py + sim_ub fixtures; loses the documented command + the future triage-oracle option); (b) FREEZE + mark experimental (current de-facto state, still pays the arm-threading tax on every GIR change and keeps rotting); (c) RE-ARCHITECT as a BIR-consuming `Backend` impl (inherits every shared-pass semantic by construction — kills the duplication objection at the root) IF the fuzzer divergence corpus ever justifies the triage oracle; (d) **evaluate first: deliver the UB-explanation feature WITHOUT an interpreter** — an instrumented native build (compiler-emitted provenance/drop checks + the existing `__gorget_current_alloc` interposition, Go-race-detector-style) shares 100% of the real pipeline and cannot drift; likely the more Gorget-shaped answer per "one source of truth per axis". Own scout before any of (c)/(d).
